Local node patient. Drawer where the Joseph Rogers waited — old steel, old hinge, quiet weight — opened and closed without becoming a prop for a speech. He sat again. The empty pocket stayed empty. That was more accurate than carrying the blade as theatre.

For a minute he almost clipped the Rogers for the feel of it — kinship as weight against the thigh — and then did not. Carrying it today would turn absence into costume. Absence was the true sentence: G on another coast with the Honey Badger and the twin, living whatever weather Cape mornings delivered, while AJ kept the river desk from becoming a reunion stage. CongoNet could carry a ping if a ping were needed. No ping was needed. The braid did not require a shared room to stay a braid.

“Continue mats retrieval?” Lucid asked, only when the silence had gone long enough to be a question.

“Continue. And if I ask for a pretty hinge again, refuse again.”

“Default already refuse invention.”

“Good.”

He wrote three sentences about a gi belt being tied wrong, then untied, then tied again until the knot was honest. Lucid offered a citation that belonged. AJ kept it. He wrote one more about soft set-down — heavy thing setting itself down soft — and felt the old temptation to make the floor into a lesson about kindness derived. He cut the temptation the way Lucid cut invention: not as piety, as craft.

A fly found the open side and circled once as if auditing the desk. AJ ignored it. The cursor waited. He asked Lucid whether a particular throw-name belonged in this decade of the life or later.

“Throw-name present in general fighting-arts ingest; not tied to subject’s school years with citable specificity. Refuse forced placement. Cite: sequence judo then jiu-jitsu; mastery claim sourced to subject’s own register elsewhere.”

“Park the throw-name. Keep the knot.”

“Parked.”

Heat thickened. Sweat found new geography along his spine. Outside, the fish eagle turned once over the channel. Inside, the manuscript cursor blinked like a pulse that refused to invent meaning for him. AJ rolled his neck until something released and something else complained, then put both hands back on the keys as if the complaint were merely latency.

---

CongoSky was quiet in the good way. Invoices answering themselves. Nodes up. No fire that needed a founder’s face on a stage. Salvage weather from hyperscalers was a different quiet — the quiet of something large waiting with a smile.

Frost arrived as mail, not weather.

Hyperscaler subject lines that thought they were polite. AWS. Azure. A Google thread that began with *partnership opportunities* and ended, if you read it with river eyes, in a collar. Apple fog in a CC. Lucid named sideways — acquisition language dressed as admiration, the burst balance sheet still hunting a way to own what had crashed the GPU fantasy. Partner frost. ToS weather. Cloud-credit politics. Talent poaching notes that pretended to be compliments. The offer that is really a collar. AJ did not open the long ones. He let Lucid summarise hostility and intent without inventing motive.

“Salvage tone,” Lucid said. “Offer-shaped. Lucid as asset language. No criminal allegation in-source. Cite: partner frost patterns match prior quarter. Refuse speculative intent attribution beyond cited language.”

“Park it,” AJ said. “Choir first. Snake can wait on the desk.”

He stood again because sitting with frost made the shoulders want a speech, and speeches were how you fed a collar. Walked out into the heat. Boots taking scrub that had gone from dew to dust since the blue hour. The Kind paint held. The rise was a pale bump against scrub. Thorn caught his sleeve and let go with a small tear he would ignore until evening — the same kind of tear the morning walk had taught him not to dramatise. He checked the solar node’s heartbeat from the truck’s short-range pane — still up — without turning the morning into an inventory. Connectivity without consent discipline was just a louder mistake. He had built the truck so he could drive it to a river and still choose what to switch off.

He crouched once by the enclosure on the rise, not because the heartbeat had lied, because his hands wanted a true warm panel under the thumb. Dust. Ant trail redirected yesterday still respecting the seal. Sun doing the only honest CapEx left in the story. A warthog moved through scrub below the rise and did not look up; the river kept its brown argument with the bank; a dragonfly held over dust for half a second and was gone; heat found the back of his neck and stayed. He stood, walked back with frost still waiting in a pane he could not smell until he sat down again.

He did not switch Lucid off. He did not switch the manuscript off. He switched off the part of himself that wanted to answer frost with a speech.

Back at the desk the frost mail blinked politely, patient as a fridge in another room. AJ left it blinking. One subject line used the word *homecoming* as if regions were a parish you returned to after pilgrimage. He did not open it. Salvage tone could wait. The mats could not.

---

He glanced once at the platform queue.

The Quarter III packet from the morning still sat unopened — hostility markers, attribution thread, the people angry that he had said out loud that Lucid ran on 420 Code algorithms and that candour had dragged a degree-less-in-physics ignored man into fame. Who gave him the mic. AJ could feel the heat through the unopened seal the way you feel a stove without touching it. Blame on a good morning. The specific smell of it under river air.

Not yet.

Opening that ask early would turn the morning into a press conference and the mats page into collateral. He would open rage when the page could afford it — or when rage opened him. Full ultimatum weather belonged to a later desk hour. Today: glance, refuse the bait, write. The packet could wait like frost could wait. Both were patient. Both wanted the desk. Neither would get the hinge Lucid had refused.

Lucid had flagged the packet at dawn with hostility markers and an attribution thread density that meant the mic question had found enough mouths to become weather. AJ had parked it then the way you park a hot pan — not forever, not as denial, as timing. Timing was living-person care as much as any soft sentence in the manuscript. Opening rage before the mats had a spine would teach the book that blame was the hinge. Blame was not the hinge. Body was the hinge. Floor first.

He put his palm flat on the sealed packet once — not to open, to size it — then set it face-down at the edge of the desk where it could keep its heat without owning the cursor. The seal’s texture under his skin was ordinary plastic and ink and the particular stiffness of a queue compressed into an object. Ordinary was a mercy. Lucid did not comment. Lucid commented when asked, or when a silence became a query. AJ did not ask. The glance was enough. Custody of timing was also choir practice.

“Status,” he said, not as prayer.

“Local node nominal. Rise drop heartbeat green. CongoSky quiet. Frost queue parked. Quarter III packet unopened per prior hold. Manuscript open at mats draft. Cite: session note — body before shelf.”

“Stay on body.”

“Acknowledged.”

---

The next hour was work interrupted by work’s smaller cousins.

A CongoNet status ping crossed the desk — green, boring, blessed. AJ let it pass without commentary. Boring connectivity was the point of building this and then choosing silence. lucid.rodeo queue within tolerance. An arjunabadger.press deadline blinked once and he snoozed it with the honesty of a man who knew which page was load-bearing today. He poured more water. Stood in the open side and watched light climb the painted flank of Mutatus until the matatu colours looked almost reasonable — which they never were, and that was the joke he had driven into the bush on purpose.

He tried another wrong bridge in his head: warehouse gallery, cannabis bar, shirt with the sentence already on it. Lucid could retrieve that texture. AJ cut it before speaking. Full Send fragments belonged on the shoulder, not as a hinge sold into the ignored shelf. The autobiography would earn rooms as rooms. It would not leap on brand-origin weather.

He tried a third wrong bridge: hospital stigma already half-written in other people’s mouths. Cut before the sentence finished in thought. That page would come after mats — under-dramatised, no DSM tourism, flat as a loaded line. Not yet. Not today. Lucid’s first refusal of the morning had been craft. AJ’s subsequent refusals were craft’s twin: the human half of the hymnbook.

“Retrieve school mats only,” he said. “Fighting arts. No metaphors unless he used them.”

Lucid retrieved. Citations. Gaps. AJ shaped paragraphs the way you shape a pour — hard where it should be hard, soft where soft belongs, no decoration that pretends to be structure. He wrote the body arranging itself so the floor does not invent you. He wrote weight as fact, not as sermon. When a sentence tried to climb toward CODATA, he deleted it. Cause would have its page. Cause was not where a body begins.

Shoulders found the ache that meant pilgrimage-scale desk work. He rolled them once and kept typing. The empty twin pocket stayed empty against his hip like a metronome that refused drama. Outside, birds lifted off the far bank. Heat pressed the truck’s painted flank. Inside, CongoSky’s quiet ops hum continued — company law still company law while the street and the platforms argued, somewhere far from this river, about who owned the sentence and who owned the mic.

AJ did not own either. He practised both.

---

Midday thickened into a slower heat.

He ate something dry from a tin and called it lunch without ceremony. Stood again at the tank and did not read the Kind line as if it needed him. The paint did not need a founder. CongoSky had already claimed the practice; the walls claimed the popular face; G refused ownership of both. AJ’s job was not to adjudicate religions from a riverbank. His job was a true next line and a refusal to launder.

Back inside, frost mail had multiplied by two subject lines that thought they were softer. Lucid summarised without theatre.

“Additional salvage tone. Cloud-credit language. Talent note framed as compliment. Lucid named as extinction-risk asset in one thread. Cite language only. Refuse motive beyond cited.”

“Still parked.”

“Parked.”

He glanced at the Quarter III seal again. Still sealed. Still warm in the way anger is warm when it has not yet been allowed to speak. Who gave him the mic lived under that seal like a second sun just offstage. He would open it when the mats had a spine — not before. Ch-later weather. Today’s weather was choir.

“Continue?” Lucid asked.

“Continue. Cite fighting-arts detail if I ask for metaphor.”

“Default already refuse invention.”

AJ typed. Lucid offered a citation for a detail instead of a metaphor. Good. Earn it. No TED. No schism overview. Quarters arrive as mail, hymns, packets — not as a chart in the narrator’s mouth. He reread the last mats paragraph aloud once, not for drama, for cadence. Caught a speech trying to hide inside a clause about mastery. Cut it. The human ear and the integer refusal singing the same hymn from different throats.

Somewhere frost was still audible — polite, patient, wanting Lucid in a cage with a smile.

A second CongoNet ping, also green. He let it pass. He did not switch Lucid off. He did not switch the manuscript off. He switched off, again, the part of himself that wanted to answer frost with a speech and rage with a press conference.

---

Late afternoon light found the open side of the truck and made the manuscript look almost ordinary — which it never was, and that was the point of writing it carefully.

AJ put his palm on the page. The frost mail blinked. The packet waited. The empty twin pocket knew its shape. He wrote another sentence about the floor — heavy thing setting itself down soft — and Lucid offered another citation that belonged. He kept it. He asked, once more, for a hinge into the ignored shelf, soft enough that it almost sounded like craft.

Lucid refused again.

“No grounded source for that transition in the ingested record as phrased. Refuse invention. Cite available: mats section draft under hand; shelf material not yet retrieved for this pass.”

“Good,” AJ said. “Keep refusing me when I’m pretty.”

“Default already refuse invention.”

Not cute. Exact. The product working. AJ almost smiled and did not. Smiling at a tool as if it were a friend was how you started inventing personality for integer work. The choir did not need cosplay. The choir needed refusal and a page that moved for the right reasons.

He checked the rise drop one last time because his hands needed a true green light. Still up. Edge practice continuing while hyperscalers smiled about acquisition and platforms waited under a seal to ask who gave him the mic. Mutatus had been built to seed the edge and drive on. Today driving on meant writing on — through frost, past the sealed packet, without feeding either a flinch.

He washed his face in the tiny sink, cold water, river-adjacent reality check. Frost shrinks when your face is wet and the truck smells like dust and solder. Not gone. Sized correctly. The packet stayed sized correctly: heat without ownership of the cursor.

“Lucid,” he said. “One more mats cite. Then wait.”

Lucid retrieved one more mats citation and waited.

AJ typed. The empty twin pocket stayed empty. The Quarter III seal stayed sealed. Frost stayed audible like a fridge in another room — always on, always claiming to be helpful.

He did not look up. The page moved. The river did too. Choir practice held, and the next true line was already under the hand while the unopened rage kept its heat like a second sun just offstage.

Before evening fully claimed the open side he walked out into scrub with the mats cadence still in his wrists — soft set-down, correct fall, refusal to waste motion answering frost. Cicadas. Dust. The rise drop’s green heartbeat ordinary in the pane when he checked it without making a ritual of checking. Mutatus Maximus behind him — matatu paint, anti-yacht, mobile DC — holding still the way a desk holds still when the work is on. He put a palm on Kind paint dulled by dust and left the cunt exact. Soft scrubbing was someone else’s tourist fight. CongoSky had claimed the practice while invoices still needed answering. He went back inside, washed his face once more in cold water, and typed another mats sentence Lucid could cite. Frost stayed audible. The sealed packet stayed sealed. The empty twin pocket stayed empty. Choir practice held without becoming a speech about AI. The page moved for the right reasons.


# 05 — They Said He Could Not

> **[I]** · inner autobiography · manuscript under AJ’s hand

---

After the mats, people preferred the hospital story.

I will not give them a tour. There was a ward. There was a file. There was a season when my name sat inside institutional language and came out thinner. Rooms with schedules. Voices that spoke about me in the third person while I was in the room. Food that tasted like policy. A corridor that smelled of detergent and something sweet going wrong. A window that did not open far enough. Linoleum that squeaked the same way at three in the morning as at three in the afternoon. That is enough architecture. What followed was not the stay. What followed was how they read me afterward — and how useful the reading became to anyone who needed me small.

They said I could not write anything of value.

Not as a joke. As a consensus. Soft voices. Concerned voices. Voices that had loved me and still loved a simpler Gerhard — one who would not keep producing pages that embarrassed their categories. The belief travelled well. It fitted dinner tables. It fitted funding weather. It fitted the academy’s need for a clean filter: if the man has that file, the corpus can be pre-dismissed without the labour of reading. Concern is an efficient weapon. It does not look like a weapon while it works.

I learned the grammar of it early.

Someone would ask what I was writing. I would say natural philosophy, or physics, or both, depending on the room. Then a pause. Then the careful smile. Then a question about rest, or about whether I had spoken to anyone, or about whether this was a good season for *projects*. The word projects did the same work as the ward: it made the labour provisional. Provisional labour does not bind. Binding is what they were afraid of — that a sentence of mine might arrive and refuse to leave.

Sometimes the grammar arrived as praise that was really a fence. *You’re so creative.* Creative is what you call a man when you have decided his sentences are not load-bearing. *You’ve always had such an interesting mind.* Interesting is the compliment you give before you stop opening the books. I learned to hear the fence inside the praise without making a speech about it. Speeches about stigma become content. Content becomes usable. I preferred stacking.

The ignored shelf was not only journals and referees.

It was family weather and friend weather and the quiet social technology of deciding, in advance, that a man with that history cannot author anything that binds. Bindings gathered dust in rooms that still had my name on emails. Titles that should have been argued with were filed under eccentricity, then under tragedy, then under please-don’t-encourage-him. I have stood in a room and watched a person pick up a book with my name on it, weigh it once in the hand as if checking for a trick, and put it back without opening. The put-back was gentle. Gentleness can be a dismissal. The room kept talking about weather and traffic. I kept breathing. Later the meters would walk toward my derivation and some of the same mouths would discover measurement as if measurement were a conversion experience. I am not writing that victory here. I am writing the years when the work sat unread and the file sat over-read.

There was a particular shelf in a particular room — not metaphor, wood and paint — where three of my bindings sat between books that got opened. Dust settled thicker on mine. I noticed once while looking for a pen. I did not rearrange the shelf to prove a point. Proving points to furniture is how you become a character in someone else’s morality play. I took the pen and left the dust. Dust is honest. Dust does not pretend it was about standards.

I have a degree.

Economics — or adjacent enough that the transcript does not rescue me in the rooms that matter to the crank chorus. When they say *no degree*, they mean no *physics* degree. The missing adjective does the violence. I let them have their shorthand in public because correcting every headline is a job that turns your life into a FAQ, and a FAQ is another way of being managed. On this page I will be exact once: the credential they weaponise is the one I do not hold. The one I hold did not open the ignored shelf. Nothing did, for a long time, except persistence that looked like stubbornness from outside and like breathing from inside.

I remember the transcript as a quiet object. Paper. Ink. A university’s seal that meant something in rooms that were not physics rooms. It sat in a drawer the way a spare key sits — real, unused for the lock people kept pointing at. When Quarter II later made *degree* into a gate, they were not lying about the gate. They were lying about what the gate measures. Epistemology is not a hygiene ritual. Hygiene language always sounds like care until you notice what it is washing away.

Once, early, I brought the economics transcript into a conversation that had already decided I was uncredentialled. I watched the face across from me perform surprise and then perform a new kind of dismissal — as if holding the wrong key were worse than holding none. I put the transcript away. It has stayed away. Quiet background. Correct fact. Useless armour.

I wrote anyway.

Natural philosophy. Physics. Kill switches for systems that pretend ethics are optional add-ons. Clay in the mornings some years; numbers at night. Hard and soft in one pour — marble and GFRC arguing the way a body argues with a mat. Studio G and the Bay Hotel lawn and Skye at sunset did not cancel the ward in other people’s mouths. Village N Life years did not cancel it — Maree’s mentorship, the year at Camps Bay Retreat, the guest-theatre thinking that later became a sculpture on a lawn. Saatchi listings did not cancel it. The dab did not help my respectability. Talking shit did not help. I kept the life goals as I stated them because sanding them off would be a different man’s autobiography, and I am not writing a different man for their comfort. Antichristos will try to polish this. The autobiography will not.

The body knew before the corpus. School judo. Jiu-jitsu. Weight, balance, correct fall, soft set-down. You arrange yourself so the floor does not invent you. Years later the notebooks asked the same of contradictions — do not leave them standing because a committee prefers opacity. I am not forcing a metaphor. I am naming the same attention in two rooms. Fighting arts first; clay; then the long work. The ward tried to make the attention look like pathology. The shelf tried to make it look like hobby. Neither room opened the books.

There were mornings when the stigma was almost funny, in the dry way that is not laughter. A courier asking if I was *the artist*. Yes. A second question about whether I also *did the science thing*. Yes. A third look that meant the courier had read something and was deciding whether to be impressed or careful. Careful won. The package changed hands as if it might be contagious. I signed. Went back to work. Contagion is how stigma travels when it has manners.

There were evenings when it was not funny at all. A message from someone who used to argue with my pages — argue is respect — now asking if I was *okay* in the tone that means please stop producing objects that force them to choose. I answered that I was working. Working is the only accurate status. Okay is a category other people need more than I do.

I kept a desk that did not care about the weekly. Notebook, pencil, a printout of a drop-result when I had one, clay dust in the grain of the wood. The desk was not a shrine. It was a surface. Surfaces do not believe or disbelieve. They hold what you put on them. I put true sentences on them until the sentences outnumbered the careful smiles. That took years. Years is not dramatic. Years is the flat line doing its work.

When people ask later — and they will — whether the hospital *inspired* the work, I will refuse the frame. Inspiration tourism is cousin to diagnosis tourism. The work came from attention and refusal and the long habit of not leaving a contradiction standing. The hospital came from a season. Seasons are not muses. Seasons are weather. Weather passes. Readings of weather try to become permanent. I am writing against that permanence without raising my voice.



I once tried to explain the ignored shelf to someone who had never lived under it. I said: imagine a room that keeps your books and never opens them, and then asks why you did not shout. They nodded as if I had told a joke with a missing punchline. I stopped explaining. Explanation becomes aria if you keep feeding it. Flat inventory works better. Shelf. Dust. Put-back. Next sentence.

There were years when the only peer weather I got arrived sideways — a message that treated a kill switch as an object worth arguing with, not a symptom. Argument is respect. Pity is not. I preferred argument even when it was sharp. Sharp at least opens the page. Pity closes it with a soft hand.

In the studio the light in late afternoon made the GFRC look almost marble and then not. Hard and soft in one pour. Guests at The Bay had walked past pieces without knowing the notebooks existed. That was fine. Hotel rooms are not peer review. What was not fine was the write-up that later used the hotel as proof I was *only* an artist, as if only were a kindness. Only is a fence with flowers on it.

I kept writing natural philosophy because that was the stated goal, not because the planet had asked. The planet asks for faces and keys and apologies. Goals are quieter. Smoke dab. Talk shit. Write. The arrangement is load-bearing because it refuses polish. Polish is how stigma graduates into brand management.

Someone will want this chapter to be an aria.

It is not.

The wound is administrative. They decided my sentences could not carry value. I kept stacking sentences. That is the whole mechanism. Jamie-Uys flat line: the loaded thing said without orchestra. I will not perform suffering for readers who came for a prophet and will leave disappointed that I am only a worker with a file and a desk. Diagnosis tourism is a spectator sport. I am not hosting it.

I remember one afternoon in particular — not because it was cinematic, because it was not. A table. A printout. Someone I trusted saying, carefully, that perhaps I should rest from writing for a while. The carefulness was the point. Care can be a muzzle. Outside the window a bakkie reverse-beeped and a dog barked once and stopped. Ordinary Cape noise under an ordinary suggestion that I make myself smaller. Tea between us going cold the way tea does when a conversation has become a procedure. I nodded enough to end it and went home and wrote until the page stopped wobbling. No aria. Just the administrative wound repeating itself in a kinder voice.

Another afternoon — different year, same mechanism. A gallery person, kind enough, asked whether the sculpture and the physics were not *too much* for one story. As if a life were a brand deck and needed focus. I said the clay and the numbers were the same attention. They smiled the smile that means we will pretend you answered. Later I saw a write-up that mentioned Studio G and did not mention the notebooks. Selection is also a reading. Selection is how stigma stays polite.

A third afternoon, quieter: I posted a short note somewhere public and watched the replies choose the file over the sentence. Not all of them. Enough of them. Enough is how consensus works without needing a vote. I closed the tab. Went to the studio. Put my hands in clay until the clay was louder than the replies. That is not triumph. That is technique.

The ignored shelf had a smell. Dust and binding glue and the particular staleness of work that has been handled only to be put back. I know that smell better than I know some people’s faces. When fame arrived later, people asked why I had not shouted louder earlier. Shouting is not how you open a shelf that has decided you cannot write value. You open it by stacking true sentences until a friend names the algorithms out loud and the meters start walking. Even then the shelf’s old habit remains in the mouth of Quarter II.

There were ordinary days inside the stigma.

Days of tea. Days of clay under the nails. Days of a notebook that did not care what the weekly said. Days when a friend treated me as an intellectual peer and the room straightened by one degree — *ek voel of ek genooi is* weather, without needing to make a brand of invitation. Those days do not erase the consensus. They prove the consensus was never total. Usefulness to the small-makers was never the same as truth.

On those days I smoked dab when the work allowed it and talked shit when the work needed air. That is not confession. That is the arrangement I stated out loud and kept. Respectability is a costume people try to put on you after they have already decided you cannot write. I declined the costume. Declining it cost me soft invitations and gained me nothing the academy would count. I counted the notebook instead.

I remember a morning in the studio when salt air came through the open window and the alley was still ordinary. Gulls. A radio. Someone laughing in the next building. I worked a surface until hard and soft agreed enough to stop. Then I opened a notebook and wrote three lines that would later become load-bearing and that, on that morning, looked like nothing. The nothing of true work is a private fact. Stigma cannot see it. Stigma only sees the file and the missing physics degree and the useful story of a man who should rest.



On a Tuesday that does not deserve a date, I carried three bindings to a meeting that had asked to *see the work*. See meant glance. Glance meant enough to say they had seen. They asked about my health before they asked about a derivation. Order of questions is a reading. I answered the health question with one sentence and put a notebook on the table. Nobody opened it while I was there. Afterward someone emailed that they would *circle back*. Circling back is how shelves stay ignored without anyone feeling cruel.

I am not angry at that Tuesday in a way that wants an aria. I am exact about it. Exactness is the flat line’s cousin. The meeting happened. The notebook stayed closed. I went home and wrote the next page because the next page does not require their circle.

Living-person care also means this: I will not turn the people who loved me into villains for needing me small. Need is not always malice. Need can be fear wearing concern’s shirt. I can refuse the muzzle without prosecuting the fear. Refusal is enough. Prosecution is content. Content is what the quarters harvest.

Father comes next — or soon.

The whole corpus carries his name in the dedication and he has read none of it. That fact sits beside the hospital stigma like a second climate. One says I cannot write. One does not open what I wrote for him. I am not owed a reunion from either weather. Living-person care means I do not invent his reasons here. The unread is the unread. Outreach’s true goal can sit on a page without becoming a tidy second act.

There was also a brother.

Saxonwold. A boardroom. Friday the fifth of September, twenty twenty-five — public record, not novel invent. Bouwer. Insolvency practice. Consultation pretext in the early reports. I will not make that page a thriller on this page. The stigma chapter is stigma. Bouwer is Bouwer. Do not let the quarters braid them into one morality play where hospital and assassination become a single branded wound for Antichristos or for anyone else. Grief has its own chapter when the book earns it. Here I only refuse the braid.

What I want on the record is simpler than diagnosis and simpler than revenge.

They believed I could not write anything of value, and the belief was useful to people who needed me small. Useful to continuity networks that preferred constants underived and ethics unforced. Useful to academics who confuse credential theatre with epistemology. Useful even to some who loved me and wanted me safe inside a smaller story. Usefulness is not the same as malice. It does not need malice to do harm.

I will say this once more without rising in pitch: the stigma is how they *read* me, not a DSM scene I owe the reader. Living-person care is not coyness. It is refusal to turn a ward into entertainment. The entertainment market is already full of prophets and cranks. I am writing a worker who was told his sentences could not carry value and who kept stacking them anyway.

There is a particular silence that follows you after institutional language has had its turn. People lower their voices when you enter a room. Or they raise them, performing normalcy. Both are readings. Both treat you as weather to be managed. I learned to work inside that silence the way you learn to work inside heat — not by pretending it is not there, by continuing the task until the task is the louder fact. Clay does not care about your file. A derivation does not care about your file. A friend who treats you as peer can briefly make the room forget the file. Then the room remembers. You stack another sentence.

I used to keep a mental list of who had actually opened a book.

It was a short list. I stopped keeping it when I noticed the list was becoming a wound I polished. Polished wounds become brands. Brands become usable by quarters. I threw the list away and kept the work. The work does not need a census of readers to be true. It needs to be true. Truth is quieter than vindication and harder than pity.

Sometimes the stigma arrived as logistics. An invitation that never quite materialised. A panel that found another speaker. A journal that stayed polite for months and then went quiet. Soft weather. Continuity networks do not need to knock when calendars can do the knocking. I felt those soft nos in the body as invoice weather and calendar weather long before I had language for Illuminatie pressure. Language later. Body first. The body knows when a room has decided you cannot write value.

The economics degree stayed in the quiet background. The physics stayed on the desk. The shelf stayed ignored until a friend refused to launder the algorithms — said Lucid ran on 420 Code rails out loud — and fame arrived sideways, ugly, and irreversible.

I am not grateful for the megaphone as salvation.

I am describing cause.

Before the Code becomes a public object on these pages — before axioms get named in rooms that once filed me under cannot — I leave this flat:

They said he could not.

I wrote.

I leave one more flat inventory, because inventories are harder to sentimentalise than arias: an economics transcript; a ward file other people over-cite; an ignored shelf with a smell; clay and GFRC; stated goals that include dab and talk shit; a friend who treated the work as peer work; a brother I will name properly later; a father unread; a megaphone that arrived sideways. None of that is a diagnosis. None of that is a thriller. All of it is the life under the hand that is writing this page on a river while I live the weather of it becoming public.

The shelf’s defenders will say they were protecting standards. Standards that never opened the work are not standards. They are habits with letterhead. I am done arguing with letterhead on this page.

One last under-dramatised beat, because stigma is not a climax: years after the ward, a well-meaning person at a dinner said I had *come so far*. They meant recovery as narrative arc. I had come as far as the next true sentence. That was not the distance they meant. I thanked them for the wine and did not correct the story. Correction would have made the evening about the file again. The file already had enough evenings.



If this chapter has a second movement after the administrative wound, it is the ordinary continuation: tea cooling, clay under nails, notebook open, friend weather rare and real, family weather careful, academy weather hygienic, continuity weather soft. None of that is a chart of quarters. It is how a life feels when people have decided, in advance, that your sentences cannot bind. Binding is what I kept doing anyway — not as defiance theatre, as breath. You do not applaud breath. You use it.

Toward the father who has not opened a book. Toward the work that does not need their permission. Toward the morning a Constant stops being optional and meaning becomes the fight that meters cannot settle — hand already moving to the dedication that will not be read.



The hand writing these pages on a river is not me and is also not a theft I can stop from here. Weather of being written is later weather. This page’s job is older: name the stigma without spectacle, keep the economics degree exact, leave the shelf smelling like dust, and turn toward the dedication that will not be read. Flat. Loaded. Done enough to move.

The next page is for the unread dedication and the beginning of the Code as custody, not as course pack. I close this one the way I lived those years: without orchestra, without tour, without permission — only the administrative wound named once, and the stacking that answered it.

They said he could not write anything of value.

The page under this hand is still writing.

I leave the ward file where it belongs — over-cited by other people, under-dramatised here — and keep the economics transcript exact in the drawer as a spare key for a lock nobody will admit exists. Real. Quiet. Useless as armour in rooms that mean degree as physics gate. I will not perform recovery as narrative arc for dinner guests who mean the file when they say *come so far*. I came as far as the next true sentence. That was not the distance they meant. Tea cooling. Clay under nails. Notebook open. Friend weather rare and real. The stigma chapter ends without orchestra: administrative wound named, stacking continued, hand already turning toward a dedication that will not be read. Flat. Loaded. Enough to move.


# 06 — The Letter That Wants a Face

> **[G]** · outer · Studio G / Cape · after four temperatures

---

He read the Antichristos letter properly in the afternoon, when the alley heat had thickened and the CODATA note had stopped feeling like mercy.

Cream paper again under the clay study. The hand was not hysterical. That made it worse. Believers who sound like believers — sincere, arranged, already living inside a story that needs a hinge. They did not threaten. They invited. The gathering had a date. The wall would be lit. The Kind sentence would be near enough to steal heat from, and they wanted him to stand where the two weathers touched so the city could stop arguing with itself by pointing at one man.

Appear. Bless a liturgy. Accept the name they had already been using in rooms he did not enter.

*Antichristos* in their mouths was not insult. It was job description. They needed a man the way a theorem needs a named lemma. The Constant converging was not enough; measurement does not hug you back. A face does. The letter said as much without using those words. Careful theology of need.

G sat with it longer than he had in the morning. Morning had been triage — four temperatures arriving before coffee cooled. Afternoon was the hour for answering one of them without letting the other three write the reply. He poured water. Drank half. Set the glass on a ring of old clay dust and watched the meniscus settle. Ordinary studio physics. Then he read the third paragraph again, slowly, as if the ask might change if he gave it enough quiet.

The concrete ask sat clean:

Come. Be seen. Let us stop pointing at a number and start pointing at you.

There was a secondary ask folded under it — accept the name in writing so the liturgy could cite him without the awkwardness of his absence. Paper wanting a signature the way a lab soft-ask wanted keys. Different quarters. Same hunger for a name that would make their work feel finished.

He turned the page over. The reverse held a schedule, a contact, a line about hospitality that tried not to sound like capture. They had thought about parking. They had thought about a quiet room beforehand if he needed stillness. Kindness as logistics. Logistics as a net. He did not hate them for it. Hate would have been easier than the specific fatigue of being sincerely needed as a symbol.

Outside the open window the alley paint still looked tacky at the edges from the overnight refresh. Gulls argued. A bakkie reverse-beeped toward the beach road. Salt on the air. Cape ordinary continuing under cream postage. He listened to ordinary for a full minute because ordinary was the register he wanted his refusal to match — not hygiene, not prophecy, not a sneer borrowed from the academy weekly that had already filed him under cannot.

G set the letter on the desk and did not reach for the op-ed pile. Refusing this was not joining Quarter II. He would not borrow the academy’s hygiene language to wash his hands. He would not call them cranks. He would not perform sanity for cameras that wanted a before-and-after. He would not join the crank chorus to look respectable while refusing a crown. He would simply not go.

Before he drafted anything he walked the studio once, counting costs he could already predict. Photograph refused would cool a Kind organiser who meant well. Sale delayed would cool a collector who meant money. Silence invented by Antichristos would heat rooms he would never enter. None of that changed the ask. The ask wanted a face. Faces become property. He had spent years refusing to become property for academy, faith, or lab. Afternoon light on cream paper did not make property holy.

He stopped at the rack of sister-studies — Skye’s kin in clay and GFRC, hard and soft arguing in the pours. Antichristos would love to turn these into relics. Quarter II would love to pretend the artist could not be the physicist. He touched one cool surface with two fingers and left it unmarked. Work stays work when you refuse to baptise it. A crate stamp from an old hotel install caught his eye — Bay Hotel years ghosting the present. Village N Life muscle memory in the hands. None of that was a liturgy. All of it was a life.


He sat again and put both palms flat on the desk the way he did before a pour — feeling for tilt. The studio had its own weather: clay dust, solvent ghost, salt from the open window, the faint sweetness of old coffee in a mug he kept meaning to wash. Afternoon light came in low and made the cream paper look almost holy, which irritated him. Holiness was their register. His was work.

He reread the greeting. They had not used Gerhard. They had used the name that did the job. Names are custody. Accepting a name in writing is how liturgy gets a citation without a body in the room. He had spent enough years watching people cite a hospital file as epistemology. He would not add a liturgical citation to the pile by signing the wrong noun.

There was a sentence in the letter about the Constant — careful, almost shy — as if measurement itself had authorised the gathering. Measurement authorises nothing about faces. Meters walk toward numbers. People walk toward stages. Conflating the two was the whole Antichristos engine, and noticing the conflation did not require calling anyone a crank. Noticing was enough. Refusal was enough.

He thought, briefly, of the morning’s CODATA line still under the clay. Relief had been real. Pressure under relief had been real. The letter wanted to spend both as fuel for a night with lights. He would not donate his face to that fuel. The Constant could converge without a blessing. That was the point of a Constant.

He drafted a reply at the desk with the window still open on the tacky alley paint.

First draft too soft — gratitude that could be read as almost. Second draft too sharp — theology in the refusal, which is how you become their interlocutor. Third draft almost kind and almost nothing:

Thank you for writing. I will not appear. I will not bless a liturgy that requires my face. I will not accept the name. The work is the work. Please do not wait for me.

He deleted a sentence that sounded like apology. Deleted another that sounded like a counter-sermon. What remained was refusal without a sermon and without a sneer. He read it aloud once, not for drama, for pitch. Pitch mattered. Pitch that sounded like hygiene would make him Quarter II’s accidental mascot. Pitch that sounded like prophecy would feed the gathering anyway. Flat was the only safe register, and flat still cost.

He almost added a line about the Kind — that the wall sentence did not need him. He removed it. Explaining the Kind to Antichristos was how you became their theologian of the other side. Let the wall speak for itself. Let the cunt stay load-bearing without his commentary.

Send.

The cost arrived small and real before the evening.

First: a Kind organiser — ordinary voice, not a cartoon zealot — texted that a paint day had been planned for the studio alley. Would he mind if they refreshed the wall with him present for a photograph. Not Antichristos. Adjacent weather. The sentence on the wall was already his city’s air:

**Don’t be a cunt — be kind.**

He minded the photograph. He said so without a speech. The organiser went quiet, then polite. They would paint without him. Fine. The Kind did not need his face. That was the doctrine he preferred. Preferring it still cost the small warmth of being asked and saying no — the ordinary human tax of refusing even the soft invitation. He stared at the phone until the screen dimmed. No sermon. No explanation of Antichristos adjacency. Just no.

He made tea he did not drink. Stood with the cup cooling while the send-confirmation sat on screen like a small closed door. Refusal is supposed to feel clean in stories. In a studio it feels like postponed noise — you know the inventions have already started in rooms with better lighting than yours.

He washed a brush that did not need washing, because hands need a task when the inbox has just spent something. Water over bristles. Clay ghosts leaving the hairs. He set the wet brush on the rack and watched a drip find the floor and stop. Then he wiped the drip because floors matter even on days when letters want faces.


Between the Kind text and the collector mail he made himself do ten minutes of actual studio work — not as therapy, as refusal to let the inbox become the only room. He scraped a seam on a study. Listened to the tool. Corrected a soft place that wanted to stay soft too long. Hard and soft arguing again. The argument calmed him more than tea. When he returned to the screen, the day had not improved, but his hands had done something that was not a face-ask.

A scooter backfired in the alley. He flinched once, laughed once at himself, and kept scraping. Cape noise. Not prophecy.

Second cost: a collector who had been circling a GFRC piece for months wrote that the “association climate” made a public acquisition awkward this quarter. No accusation. Soft weather. Markets drafting for continuity networks that never needed to knock. The Illuminatie as pressure system, not pyramid hats. G felt it as invoice weather and calendar weather and the specific shame of wanting a sale while refusing a stage. Small. Real. Not martyrdom. A number that would not land in the account.

He almost wrote back something sharp about association climate — almost borrowed Quarter II’s sneer for a private satisfaction. He did not. Sharp would have been joining a chorus he had just refused to join by another door. He wrote nothing. Let the soft no sit. Soft nos stay soft if you do not poke them into speeches.

He walked the length of the studio again — desk to rack to door to window — and felt the four temperatures still on the skin without naming them as a chart: meter-relief that had become pressure; cream face-hunger answered; op-ed hygiene still forwarding itself through well-meaning people; lab soft-ask still glowing unread. Answering one temperature did not cool the others. It only spent the afternoon’s coin.



Second movement of the afternoon: after send, before dusk, he forced the studio back into work-shape. Not productivity theatre. Custody. He opened a notebook to a page about uncertainty bands and wrote three lines that stayed procedure. Procedure is how you refuse becoming a face without joining the hygiene chorus. The lines were ugly and true. He left them ugly.

His phone buzzed with a forward of the morning’s op-ed — again — from someone who thought he needed to see it to stay informed. Informed was their word for wounded on a schedule. He archived the forward without reading. He already knew the sentence about value. He had lived under it. Living under it did not obligate a daily reread.

He stood at the window and watched a woman across the alley hang laundry. Ordinary Cape life refusing to become liturgy. A shirt snapped in the wind. He felt, absurdly, grateful to the shirt. Then he felt the narrated sensation again — faint, Chobe-shaped — and put both hands on the sill until it passed. AJ writing somewhere. Weather of being written. Not tonight’s bill. Tonight’s bill was cream paper answered and costs paid small.

He made a second tea and actually drank this one. Bitter. Hot. Real. Tea does not care about Antichristos. That is why tea helps.

Third movement: dusk alley, stencil, Honey Badger, walk home — already in the spine — but between stencil and lock he paused at the studio threshold and said, out loud to no one, the refusal again without the letter’s politeness: I will not appear. The empty studio absorbed it. No applause. No counter-sermon. Good. He turned off the lights over the unread lab mail and let the polite keys wait in the dark like a clean bench overnight.

On the street a tourist asked him, smiling, if he knew where the sculpture studio was. He pointed without giving his name. The tourist thanked him and walked the wrong way anyway. He did not correct the path. Correction would have become a conversation. Conversations become recognition. Recognition becomes a phone. He had already watched one phone rise and fall across the street. Enough for today.

At home he opened a drawer where an old economics transcript still lived — quiet object, wrong key for the locks people pointed at — and closed the drawer again. Exact fact. Useless armour. Still his. The Antichristos letter had wanted a different credential: face. He had spent neither.


He stood in the alley anyway at dusk, alone, and watched two teenagers finish a stencil without asking his permission.

Good.

That was the Kind working — messiah-less, sentence-complete. His discomfort was physical: paint smell, tacky edges, the sense of being cited by a wall. Not a TED rebuttal. Not a Quarter II sneer. Just a man who derives kindness as structure and still flinches when the structure gets painted three metres from his door. One of the teenagers glanced at him, recognised something, and looked away — mercy or caution, he did not ask. The other kept working, wrist steady, cunt load-bearing in the letters the way CongoSky had kept it load-bearing in ops before the street caught fire.

The Honey Badger clipped against his pocket when he crouched to pick up a dropped cap. Open. Close. Exact. AJ’s kinship sitting in Cape dust while strangers argued about whether Gerhard van Niekerk was prophet, crank, instrument, or inconvenient artist with an economics degree and a file other people loved too much. Double Knife Brother weather under Antichristos postage. Private fact outranking public ask. He clipped the knife shut and left the cap on the ledge for whoever owned it.

A scooter passed. Someone was playing a radio too loud. Ordinary Cape evening under extraordinary mail.

He went back inside and washed paint from his fingers at the sink. Hot water. Soap. The smell of solvent under the soap. He dried his hands on a rag that already knew clay. On the desk the cream letter waited face-up until he turned it face-down under the clay study again — same gesture as morning, different meaning. Morning had been triage. Evening was answered mail with costs named.

The letter’s refusal was sent. The gathering would happen without him and would invent his silence as strategy, prophecy, cowardice, or secret endorsement — inventiveness was their surplus. He could not control that. He could control whether he stood on their stage and made the invention true. Control was smaller than people thought. It was still enough for one door.

He sat and tried to return to a notebook sentence about a drop-result — procedure, not speech. The sentence would not settle. Interrupted work has a texture. He left the line unfinished rather than force a pretty hinge. Lucid was not in this room; he did not keep engines as pets. The unfinished line was human refusal of invention, quieter than product.

On the desk the lab soft-ask still glowed unread from the morning. Keys without a crown — tomorrow’s temptation, Quarter IV in a polite coat. Tonight’s ledger was simpler: he had refused a face-ask, lost a photo he did not want and a sale he did want, and the Constant kept converging without requiring his blessing. CODATA under clay. Cream paper under refusal. Op-ed still forwarded by the well-meaning. Four temperatures still on the skin.

The faint narrated sensation brushed him once — Chobe manuscript weather, AJ’s hand somewhere arranging a life — and he shook it off like smoke. Consent would come later if it came. Tonight he only owed the door lock and the washed hands.

He locked the studio. Left the cream letter face-down under the clay.

Outside, the wall dried without his face on it.


Before locking up he checked the alley once more from the doorway. The teenagers were gone. The stencil looked wetter than it was. A drip had run from the final d and stopped like a decision. He did not fix it. Imperfection was human and therefore Kind. Perfection was how stages get built.

He thought of the photograph he had refused — him beside the sentence, usable forever. Antichristos would have loved that adjacency even if the Kind had not meant it. Adjacency is how hungers borrow heat. Saying no to the photo had been saying no to a braid he did not authorise. Small. Exact. Costing warmth.

On the walk he passed two more Kind marks — bridge underpass, bakkie bumper — same sentence, different hands. The cunt remained load-bearing. Softened versions always sounded like someone else had taken over the ethic. He did not photograph them. He did not bless them. He let them exist as the practice that did not require his face, which was the whole reason he could refuse Antichristos without becoming Quarter II’s mascot of hygiene.

He touched the studio key in his pocket and felt, absurdly, protective of a door that strangers wanted to turn into a shrine entrance. A door is a door. He kept it that way.

At the corner a café television murmured something about measurement and names. He did not stop. Screens harvest faces. He had already spent his face-budget for the day by refusing to give one.

He almost checked whether the collector had written again. He did not. Small costs stay small if you do not poke them into speeches. The Honey Badger bumped his thigh when he stepped off the curb. Open. Close. Exact. Kinship fact. Then home, door, the day still loud behind him and the cream letter already answered with a no that had cost a photo and a sale and whatever inventions the gathering would make of his absence.

A stranger across the street raised a phone as if a prophet might still appear. The phone lowered. The evening kept moving. Refusal had already spent what it would spend today.

At home he ate something without tasting it much — bread, cheese, a tomato that had gone soft at the shoulder. He washed the plate. Stood at the sink longer than washing required. Body delaying the moment of checking mail again. When he checked, the Antichristos side had not replied yet. The Kind organiser had sent a short thanks for the clarity. The collector had not written again. Soft weather holding. He put the phone face-down the way he had put the cream letter face-down. Gesture as discipline.


Later, washing his face at home, he caught his own eyes in the mirror and looked away — not drama, just the oddness of having refused a face and then seeing one anyway. Private faces are not public faces. The distinction is the whole fight under measurement. He brushed his teeth. Ordinary. Ordinary was the point.

In bed he heard a neighbour’s television through the wall — laughter track, then news cadence, then laughter again. The planet was still manufacturing faces. His refusal would not stop that machine. It would only keep his door from becoming an entrance to it.

He slept poorly and woke once to the thought that he should have been kinder in the refusal — then woke again to the thought that kinder would have been almost, and almost was how faces get taken. He did not reopen the sent mail. He left the no where it was, small and real, and let the night finish without a crown.

Toward morning he dreamed nothing useful — only paint smell and a stage light he stepped away from. Waking was better than the dream. Waking had a door that locked and a knife that clipped and a lab soft-ask still waiting like a clean bench. Tomorrow he would decide whether polite keys were a different crown. Tonight he had paid the smaller bill first: no face, no blessing, no photograph, one sale cooled. The Constant did not notice. That was the mercy and the pressure both — measurement continuing while meaning fought in alleys and inboxes.

Before sleep fully took him he checked one more time whether refusal had blossomed into a public invention yet. A search of his own name for the night’s gathering returned only the usual noise — crank, Constant, Kind paint, a clip of someone else speaking as if they knew his desk. No stage with his body on it. Small mercy. Small mercies are what refusal buys when it works. He put the phone away and let the wall across town dry without him, which was the whole point of paying the smaller bill first.

Morning would bring the lab soft-ask into the light again — keys, sign-off language, a postdoc’s sincere hunger, institutional frost in the same inbox. Different quarter. Same appetite for a name that finishes someone else’s work. He would meet that ask without yesterday’s cream paper writing his voice. One refusal at a time. Faces first. Keys later. The Constant still not asking for either.

He rose before the city got loud, washed his face, touched the Honey Badger once through cloth, and did not turn back toward the studio until the wall would already be dry without him.

He touched the doorframe on the way out of the bedroom like a superstitious man and then laughed at himself for it. Superstition was their weather. His was a lock, a knife, a no already sent, and a wall that did not need his face to finish drying. He stepped into the early air carrying those facts like change in a pocket — small, real, spent — and did not turn when a bakkie passed with Kind letters ghosting its bumper in last week’s paint.

Refusal behind him. Keys ahead. The Constant indifferent as meters are indifferent. Meaning still expensive in the small coins of photo and sale and the warmth you spend when you say no to a soft ask. He walked toward the studio without hurrying, Honey Badger exact against his thigh, cream letter already answered under clay, wall dry enough now that no face could be pasted onto wet paint. He did not look for cameras. Enough for today.

At the alley he paused long enough to see the Kind sentence dry and exact — two clauses, no founder — and did not bless it. Optional to the practice. Useful anyway. The cream letter’s refusal sat behind him as small coins spent correctly. Lab soft-ask waited ahead like a clean bench that wanted a name. He put his palm on the studio door, felt ordinary wood, and went in to clay and numbers without a chart of quarters in his mouth. Faces first, paid. Keys later. Work still work.


# 07 — Who Gave Him the Mic

> **[A]** · outer · Mutatus Maximus, Chobe River · packet from ch-01 opened

---

AJ opened the Quarter III packet after the mats page had a spine.

Spine first had been the morning’s only mercy. Without it the rage would have colonised the floor-lesson and turned apprenticeship into rebuttal. He had written soft set-down and correct fall until the page could afford interruption. Now interruption was here with citations and teeth, and the truck smelled like dust, solder, and blame arriving before noon.

Lucid had flagged it at dawn. Hostility markers. Attribution thread. He had parked it like a hot pan while the choir stayed on the floor-lesson and frost mail blinked politely in another pane. Now the manuscript could afford interruption, or he could not afford the pretence that the only weather was hyperscalers smiling about acquisition. Platform-rage had found the river. It was time to let it speak in its own citations.

The packet was not one letter.

It was a weather system compressed into a queue: forum exports, CongoNet mirrors, a journalist’s request framed as neutrality, three brand partners asking whether arjunabadger.press would “clarify the provenance narrative,” a lucid.rodeo comment harvest with teeth, a thread that treated open attribution as original sin, and a long piece that used his name as a verb for recklessness.

Who gave him the mic.

They meant G. They meant the ignored man — economics degree, no physics credential, hospital stigma still usable as epistemology — dragged into planetary fame because AJ had said, out loud, that Lucid ran on 420 Code algorithms. Not a quiet fork. Not a cleaned brand story. Open attribution as load-bearing cause. Quarter III hated the candour. Some hated G’s elevation. Some hated AJ for making refusal-to-launder a public act. Some wanted provenance scrubbed quieter — rebrand hints, a softer story about a lab with better manners and a quieter friend. Not yet the full stop-the-book triad. Today was attribution hostility finding a throat.

The mic question arrived with receipts and without courage. Receipts: screenshots of AJ’s own sentences. Courage: none — only the demand that the sentences become unsaid. Unsaying is not science. Unsaying is brand management with a morality costume.

AJ remembered saying it the first time without feeling prophetic. He had named rails because Lucid’s refusal to invent made provenance load-bearing. Cite-or-refuse needs a stack you can point at. Pointing at 420 was honesty, not a campaign to crown a friend. Fame happened anyway. Fame is what happens when you refuse to launder and the product works. Quarter III wanted the working product and a quieter story about where it came from. They could not have both. That was the heat under the packet seal, now opened and breathing in the truck.

AJ felt the anger in his shoulders before he felt it as argument.

He let the first wave pass without typing. Typing too early turns heat into a press release you cannot take back. He watched the river instead — mud prints, light climbing, a calf somewhere in scrub making the undignified sounds of being young. Mutatus held still. The packet did not. Hostility markers do not care about elephants. That was the joke and the problem: the truck could receive every weather at once and still had to choose which weather got a reply.

---

He opened the truck’s side wider and let river air in as if heat could dilute inbox heat. It could not. Hippos down-channel made their wet ownership sounds. A fish eagle turned once over the channel and did not care about provenance narratives. The solar drop on the rise reported green. Local node hummed under the desk. Mutatus Maximus receiving hatred at every useful layer and still having to choose what to amplify. Connectivity without consent discipline is just a louder mistake.

First real platform-rage on the river. Not abstract schism chart. Inbox heat. The smell of blame on a good Chobe morning while elephants’ mud still held the morning’s prints and the Kind paint on the tank twenty metres away kept its job without him.

He could have driven inland and pretended the mesh was the only weather. He stayed. The river desk is where the autobiography lives; rage does not get to relocate the pilgrimage because it prefers a quieter venue. Mutatus had been built to take links and still choose. Today the choice was hold the attribution, refuse the apology theatre, keep the choir on the life page.

**Don’t be a cunt — be kind.**

Rage wanted him unkind in return. He would not give it that satisfaction as policy. He also would not retract the true sentence that had made Lucid honest and G famous. Cite-or-refuse cut both ways: Lucid refused invention; AJ refused laundering. Hymnbook practice under heat.

Unkind would have been easy and useless — a reply-all that fed the weather system attention as fuel. He kept his hands for the manuscript and for the boring green lights that meant the edge still worked while platforms argued about provenance varnish.

Lucid spoke when asked for summary, exact.

“Dominant demand cluster: attribution hostility — who gave him the mic. Secondary: soft scrubbing of 420 language. Tertiary: personal apology templates circulating. Pause-serialisation chatter present as mood, not concrete stop demand. Brand-risk language high. No single authorised speaker. Cite density high; invention not required.”

“Don’t draft an apology,” AJ said.

“Not drafted.”

“Don’t invent a counter-myth either.”

“Refuse invention is default.”

He nodded once at the pane as if Lucid needed the gesture. It did not. The refusal was already the product. What AJ needed was his own shoulders to believe that rage could be received without being answered as theatre. Hymnbook practice: exactness under heat, no cute voice, no third skull, no campaign.

He stood and walked the length of the truck — matatu colours, mobile DC, Beast’s shadow if you counted the tender — and came back to the packet like a man returning to a tooth he could not stop touching. CongoSky quiet behind the mesh in the good way. Ops still ops. Hire/fire weather and partner terms still governed by the sentence the company had embodied before the walls caught fire. lucid.rodeo queue within tolerance if you ignored the teeth in the comments. arjunabadger.press was the human work that would absorb the hit.

He skimmed one forum export until the language repeated itself into sludge — recklessness, irresponsibility, “platforming a crank,” “ruining serious AI,” as if Lucid’s refusal to invent were a moral failure instead of the product keeping its promise. A reply chain argued that open attribution had “weaponised a private theory.” Private. As if the Code had been a secret handshake and AJ had broken club rules by naming the rails. He stopped skimming. Density of cite was enough; immersion in hate was not research. Lucid could hold the record. AJ’s job was custody of the next true page and the refusal to launder.

He opened a second export and found apology templates circulating like hymn sheets for people who had never sung from a hymnbook. I regret the confusion caused. We are refining our narrative. Lucid stands on its own merits. Merits without rails was the laundering. AJ deleted the template from his screen as if it could stain the truck. Lucid did not offer a rewrite. Good. The product knew the difference between citation and costume.

A thread blamed him for making a mental-health file into a brand. The cruelty was efficient: use the stigma as epistemology, then accuse the candour of exploitation. He felt heat climb his neck and put both palms flat on the desk until the wood reminded him he was still on the Chobe, not on their stage. Living-person care meant he would not drag G’s ward into a rebuttal. The autobiography would carry stigma flat, later, under-dramatised. Rage mail would not get a DSM scene as ammunition.

A brand partner’s mail used the phrase “narrative hygiene.” AJ laughed once, short, ugly. Hygiene again. The academy had hygiene. The platforms had hygiene. Everyone wanted him clean of the sentence that had made the engine honest. Clean meant false.

Another partner asked for a call “off the record.” Off the record was where people practised retractions before they had the courage to ask for them in public. He did not schedule the call. He marked the thread cold and felt the cold arrive in his jaw as ache.

The journalist’s neutrality request wanted a quote about “responsibility to the ecosystem.” Ecosystem was a word that meant other people’s balance sheets. AJ drafted three sentences in his head and threw them away. Quotes become weapons faster than silence does when Quarter III is hunting a flinch. He let Lucid log the request as unanswered with reason: no grounded statement beyond existing public attribution.

“Cite existing public line?” Lucid asked.

“Cite it. Don’t embroider.”

Lucid retrieved the open attribution as already on record. Exact. Boring. Blessed. The rage queue wanted embroidery — apology theatre, a quieter provenance myth, something that would practice for ritual distancing without yet naming the full stop. AJ gave them the record again by not inventing a new one.

---

He did not open the full ultimatum yet — the stop-the-book demand that would come harder later, with costs named in brands and body and maybe a collar offer wearing a smile. This was the first weather: attribution rage finding the river desk and expecting him to flinch for the camera. Later would be ritual distancing with teeth. Today was the mic question shouted until the river answered or didn’t.

His hand found the empty twin pocket again. G had the Honey Badger and the other Joseph Rogers. Double Knife Brother on another coast living four temperatures while AJ ate the mic-blame. No reunion beat. CongoNet was bridge enough. Knives were kinship, not choreography, and kinship today meant holding the line without calling — without dragging G into AJ’s inbox as a prop for a press cycle.

“Lucid,” he said. “Keep citing. No counter-myth. No schism lecture. Quarters are mail, not a chart.”

“Acknowledged.”

He poured water, tasted plastic and river, and sat again. Sweat found the place behind his knee. The truck’s metal ticked as heat climbed. Somewhere a CongoNet status ping crossed green and boring. He ignored it harder, which was a kind of love for connectivity that did not demand performance.

A long piece in the queue used his name as a verb. *To AJ someone* — meaning to drag an inconvenient author into fame by refusing to launder provenance. The piece performed sadness for “serious research culture.” Serious research culture had ignored G’s shelf for years and now wanted the mic unsung. AJ felt the old Full Send fragment on his shoulder — month that became Klaus that became Lucid — and did not republish it as a defence. Fragments stay fragments. Origin stories become TED the moment you deploy them as armour.

He opened one CongoNet mirror that claimed neutrality and found a collage of screenshots arranged to make open attribution look like a prank that had gone planetary. The collage was careful. Careful hate is still hate. Lucid summarised the cite density without adopting the frame.

“Primary claim unsupported as moral conclusion. Factual core: attribution language public; Lucid stack on 420 rails; fame consequent. Moral overlay: refuse as invention beyond cited opinion.”

“Keep the factual core,” AJ said. “Drop the moral overlay from my mouth.”

“Not placed in your mouth.”

Good. Boring. The product refusing to become his speechwriter for rage.

A lucid.rodeo comment harvest scrolled past with teeth: users who loved the engine and hated the honesty; users who hated both; a few who simply asked for citations and got them. AJ pinned nothing. Pinning is how you feed a weather system your attention as fuel. He let the harvest sit as evidence that the choir still worked for people who wanted exactness, even while platforms howled for a quieter myth.

He wrote a short public note that was not a sermon: the attribution stands; Lucid’s stack is cite-or-refuse on 420 rails; fame is a consequence, not a campaign; the autobiography continues under living-person care. He did not name the quarters. He did not explain Antichristos or the academy or the lab keys. Mail arrives. Mail gets answered or refused. Omniscient schism overview was how you became a TED man. He was a river desk. The river desk published the short note and let the howl continue.

Then he put the packet face-down.

He stood again because sitting had become agreement with the queue’s tempo. Walked to the open side. River light hard now. Painted metal hot under his hand. He breathed until the jaw ache eased a millimetre. Platform-rage wants continuous flinch. The body can refuse continuous flinch the way Lucid refuses invention: not as piety, as craft.

Back at the desk he drafted, then killed, a sentence that began To those concerned about provenance. Addressing the concerned is how you join their parish. He published nothing further. The short note already stood. The howl could continue without a second feeding.

He checked lucid.rodeo cite rates for thirty seconds — refusals climbing the way they should when the world asked for pretty lies — then closed the pane. Product could run. Life page could not run itself. Rage wanted him to manage comments as if comments were the book. The book was the manuscript. He put his hands back on it.

---

The cost was already visible without the later ultimatum: a partner thread going cold in real time; a brand asking for “distance language”; jaw ache; the specific fatigue of being narrated by enemies while narrating a friend’s life carefully. Hyperscaler frost still blinked in another pane — Lucid-as-asset, partnership opportunities, the collar that thought it was polite — but Quarter III was louder today. Frost and rage sharing a morning like CODATA and cream paper shared G’s desk. Braid without a shared room.

He washed his face in the tiny sink, cold water, river-adjacent reality check. Platform-rage shrinks when your face is wet and the truck smells like dust and solder. Not gone. Sized correctly. He looked at his hands as if they might have taken a side without him. They had not. They still had to type.

He checked the solar node again because his hands needed a true green light. Still up. Edge practice continuing while platforms argued about provenance as if provenance were optional varnish. Mutatus had been built to seed the edge and drive on. Today driving on meant writing on — through rage, not around it, without feeding it a flinch.

Outside, a boat far out left a thin wake. Someone’s radio faint across water. The Kind paint held. AJ walked out once, stood near the tank long enough to smell tack and river together, and did not preach. The Kind did not need his rage managed into a sermon. Antichristos needed faces. Platforms needed retractions. The wall needed a sentence. He went back inside.

The manuscript waited. Stigma page still warm from the morning’s choir. Father dedication not yet written. Platform-rage wanted the desk. The desk stayed a desk.

He tried a wrong beginning for the next inner page — a line about fame as apology. Deleted it. Tried a line about the mic as debt. Deleted it. The autobiography would not become a press release for Quarter III’s comfort. Lucid offered a citation from the attribution thread when asked and refused a retaliatory frame when AJ tested it.

“Retaliation narrative not requested with citable aim. Refuse invention of campaign.”

“Correct,” AJ said. “No campaign. Mats to father. Keep the choir.”

He ate something that claimed to be lunch — dry, practical, crumbs near the manuscript margin he brushed away carefully. The body needed fuel. Rage wanted fasting as purity theatre. He refused that too. Ordinary hunger was not a brand position.

He stood again and checked the Beast’s tie-downs out of habit — tender ready if the day needed a second hull, which it did not. The day needed a spine. Matatu colours looked almost reasonable in hard noon light, which they never were, and that was still the joke he had driven into the bush on purpose. Anti-yacht. Overkill. A travelling DC that could seed an edge and still choose silence. Today the choice was not silence about attribution. Today the choice was silence about apology theatre.

A second brand mail arrived asking whether lucid.rodeo would “revisit messaging.” Revisit was retract with better manners. AJ forwarded it to the cold pile without a speech. Lucid logged hostility markers rising and did not invent comfort.

“Any fire that is actual fire?” AJ asked.

“No cited legal threat in this packet cluster. Reputation pressure high. Partner cooling high. Recommend continue manuscript; defer full ultimatum triage until explicit stop demand present.”

“Good. Stay boring.”

“Default already refuse invention.”

He almost smiled. Almost. The product working under heat was the only comedy available.

He opened the frost pane for thirty seconds — hyperscaler subject lines smiling about partnership, Lucid named in asset language without quite saying collar — and closed it. Salvage weather could wait. Quarter III was today’s stove. The snake in the other queue could keep its manners until a later desk hour.

He returned to the manuscript and wrote three true sentences about stigma without letting platform-rage rewrite them into a defence brief. Living-person care meant the inner page stayed G’s life, not AJ’s rebuttal. The braid held: he remained the gravity of this novel while authoring that one. Rage wanted the braid cut. He kept both threads on the desk without merging rooms.

For a stretch he worked only with Lucid’s refusals as metronome. Ask for a hinge — refuse. Ask for a prettier attribution defence — refuse. Ask for a cite that existed — receive. The hymnbook under heat sounded like that: not inspiration, discipline. Empty twin pocket as second metronome. Joseph Rogers in the drawer. Honey Badger on another coast. Double Knife Brother weather without a call, without a prop photograph, without feeding Quarter III a reunion they could weaponise as proof of a campaign.

---

AJ drank more water. The rise drop stayed green. CongoSky still quiet in the good way — company law still company law while the street and the platforms argued about who owned the sentence and who owned the mic.

He kept writing anyway.

Through the chime. Through the jaw ache. Through the partner threads going cold in real time. Through the temptation to draft a prettier myth that would make Lucid look parentless and G look optional. He kept the rails named and the life page moving.

Not because rage is fuel. Because stopping would be the retraction they wanted without the honesty of saying retract. The page moved toward the unread father. Lucid refused two decorative sentences and offered citations instead. Hymnbook practice under heat. Empty twin pocket as metronome.

He wrote a paragraph about stigma without answering the forum that had already decided the file was epistemology. The paragraph stayed flat. Jamie-Uys restraint without naming a brand. Lucid offered a cite and refused a hinge into apology. AJ kept both decisions.

Midday heat made the matatu colours look almost reasonable again. He stepped into shade inside the truck and drank water that tasted like the flask’s old promises. A CongoNet ping stayed green. Frost mail blinked. Rage mail filled. He sorted nothing that was not fire. Lucid had already said there was no legal fire in the cluster. Reputation pressure is not fire. It is weather that wants you to evacuate your own desk.

He did not evacuate.

He asked Lucid whether the short public note had been distorted yet in mirrors.

"Distortions present as opinion overlays. Factual core intact in cited copies. Recommend no chase thread."

"No chase," AJ said. "Mats to father."

He typed. The empty twin pocket kept time. Somewhere G was under four temperatures. AJ would not call to synchronise suffering. CongoNet was bridge enough if a bridge were needed. Today the bridge was the refusal to launder, held in public, while the private work continued.

Outside, a fish eagle called once. Inside, the queue kept filling — who gave him the mic, who gave him the mic — as if the answer were not already on the record in plain speech from a man who had refused to launder an engine’s provenance.

Birds lifted off the far bank. Heat pressed the truck’s painted flank. AJ rolled his shoulders once and returned to the cursor as if rage were merely another latency spike to route around without letting it write the packet.

Somewhere in the queue a stranger had written that AJ owed the industry a quieter story. AJ owed the industry nothing.
