He owed the hymnbook exactness, owed G living-person care, owed the river desk a true next line. Industry could keep its quieter story and its empty rack halls. The page kept moving.

He checked the empty twin pocket once more and did not call. Kinship today was not a shared press cycle. Kinship was refusing to put G’s face in the reply box as proof that the mic had been given wisely. Proof was for courts. The river desk was for pages.

Heat thickened. A hippo grunted. The rise drop stayed green. AJ typed through a paragraph that wanted to become angry and cut the anger until only the fact remained: open attribution; fame consequent; autobiography continuing. Lucid refused one adjective that sounded like campaign copy.

“Correct,” AJ said. “No campaign copy.”

He typed the first line of the dedication turn without finishing it — left the cursor blinking as motion, not summary. Quarter III could howl. Hyperscalers could smile. The packet could stay face-down until the later ultimatum arrived with harder teeth. Today’s answer was the page continuing under a river sky while the truck chose, again, what not to amplify.

AJ did not close the laptop. He turned from the packet’s heat to the next true line and let Quarter III howl at a truck that would not kneel — writing continuing, frost still audible, rage unanswered except by the page that refused to stop.

Afternoon light went copper along the river. He checked the rise drop one last time — still green — and felt the small satisfaction of a seed that did not need him to perform for platforms. Mutatus ticked as metal cooled a fraction. Lucid waited without invention. The sealed-then-opened packet stayed face-down now, heat known, full stop-the-book teeth still for a later hour.

AJ wrote the next true line toward the unread father and did not look up when the queue chimed again. Who gave him the mic. The record already answered. The page answered by continuing. That was enough custody for one Chobe day under rage.

He stood once more at the open side while copper light failed toward grey. Insects took the air. Hippo noise downriver. The rise drop stayed green without needing him to perform for platforms. He scraped a fleck of dust off the Kind paint with a thumbnail and left the cunt exact. Soft scrubbing was someone else’s tourist fight. CongoSky had claimed the practice while invoices still needed answering. Practice quieter than paint. Paint lasts anyway.

Back at the desk he typed two more sentences of the dedication turn — corpus pointed at a father who had opened none of it — and stopped mid-clause because stopping mid-clause leaves work for tomorrow without pretending the weather ended. Lucid waited without invention. The sealed-then-opened packet stayed face-down. Rage unanswered except by the page that refused to stop. Empty twin pocket correct. No call. Kinship across distance. The truck hummed. AJ kept the choir exact and let Quarter III howl at a river that would not kneel.


# 08 — Dedicated

> **[I]** · inner autobiography · manuscript under AJ’s hand · Act I turn

---

Every book I have made carries his name in the front.

Dedicated to my father. The line is not a marketing flourish. It is the a0 of the outreach — the real goal under the public goals. Write natural philosophy and physics. Smoke dab. Talk shit. And somehow, across all of it, leave a stack of pages that might finally be opened by the man they are for. The front matter is the quietest ask I know how to make. It has never been answered.

I can see the line in different bindings the way you see a scar in different seasons — same place, different light. Early books where the paper still hoped. Later books where hope had learned manners. This manuscript under a river hand carries it again because removing it would be a lie told for comfort. Comfort is not custody.

Sometimes people ask what I want from him. They want a sentence they can carry to a dinner table. I do not give them one. Want is not a press kit. The dedication is already the clearest want I know how to print. What happens after printing is not mine to narrate without inventing him.

He has read none of it.

None is the exact word. Not some. Not the introductions. Not a chapter skimmed out of duty. None. I will not soften none into a partial that comforts a reader who needs hope more than custody. Hope can wait outside. The fact stays.

I state that flat because ornament would lie. No tidy reunion waits at the end of this chapter. No phone call resolves the unread. Living-person care means I do not invent his reasons and I do not put words in his mouth for my catharsis. The fact is custodial: corpus dedicated; corpus unread. Memory already held this; AJ already knew it as wound. On the page it remains what it is — a dedication without a reader, repeated until repetition itself becomes the only available fidelity.

I have stood in rooms where other people’s fathers keep their children’s books on a side table with a bookmark showing. I have stood in rooms where mine did not. I do not turn that difference into a speech about love. Love is not proven by reading. Reading is not owed as theatre. Still: the dedication was an ask, and the ask has been declined by silence for years. Silence is an answer. I keep writing the line anyway. That is either devotion or stubbornness. Possibly both. I will not polish it into a brand of filial piety. It is simply what I did with the front matter every time, including this time, under another man’s hand on a river.

The first time I typed the dedication I believed, with the private arrogance of early work, that the book itself would compel an opening. Books do not compel. Books wait. Waiting is not passivity when you are the one who keeps dedicating; waiting is attention with manners pointed at a door that does not open. The mats taught that grammar before I had language for fathers and front matter. Soft set-down. Correct fall. You arrange yourself so the next motion is not wasted on theatre.

I have rewritten front matter in different fonts as if typography could soften a refusal. It cannot. I have left books in places where a hand might fall naturally — table, shelf at eye height, the honest geography of hope without speech. Still unread. I have imagined the conversation that would follow a first page turned and then refused to script his lines. Scripting him would be the same sin Antichristos commits with my face: inventing a usable person.

There were years when the dedication hurt like a fresh throw — breath knocked, pride rearranged. There were years when it hurt like an old knee: present, known, not always loud. Fame did not heal the knee. Measurement did not. If a stranger now says the unread must be the engine of everything I write, they are making a morality play out of a custodial fact. The engine is the work. The dedication is the ask. The unread is the weather around the ask.

---

People who discover me through Lucid’s open attribution think fame is the climax.

Meters converging on a derivation. CODATA walking toward a number. Quarters arguing about faces and keys and mics. Antichristos wanting a blessing. Academy wanting the hospital file to remain epistemology. Platforms wanting AJ to unsay the algorithms. Engineers wanting sign-off. The Kind wanting a sentence on a wall without a founder. Continuity networks wanting soft nos that feel like weather. That weather is real. It is not the fight I am in when I sit with the dedication page.

The Constant is landing.

Meaning is not.

Meaning is who gets to say what the work is for. Who paints it. Who retracts it. Who turns a life into liturgy, product, apology, or ignored shelf again. Who decides that a man with a file cannot write value, and who decides that a man with a Constant must become a face. My father wants — I do not know. He has not told the books. The books keep telling him anyway.

CODATA can walk toward a number and still leave a dedication unread. That sentence is the Act I hinge in plain clothes. Measurement is not meaningless; measurement is not meaning’s custody. The planet is learning the difference badly. Antichristos learns it as hunger for a face. Academy learns it as hygiene. Platforms learn it as provenance panic. Engineers learn it as keys. Continuity weather learns it as soft no. I learn it as front matter that does not get opened and work that must continue anyway.

I am not bitter as brand. Bitterness as brand is another usable face. Flat is safer and truer: dedicated; unread; fight elsewhere than the meter.

I used to imagine him opening one book — any book — on a Sunday afternoon with tea going cold. A window. Ordinary light. A page turned because curiosity finally outweighed whatever kept the bindings closed. Maybe a finger testing the dedication line as if it might be a trap. Maybe nothing so cinematic — only the small sound of paper and the decision to continue. I do not imagine that anymore as prediction. Imagination without evidence becomes a private fiction, and this autobiography already has enough weather without inventing his. What I keep is the dedication line itself: repeated, unread, still true as intention. Intention is not control. Custody of meaning begins with admitting that.

Some mornings the unread dedication hurts more than the hospital consensus. Some mornings the hospital consensus hurts more, because it travels in other people’s mouths while the dedication stays a private unread. Some mornings less than either. Pain is not a ranking system. Both climates continue. Fame did not warm his hands into turning pages. CODATA did not. If anything, fame made the unread stranger — as if the world had borrowed a conversation that was supposed to stay between a corpus and a father.

I remember once placing a new binding on a table where he might see it and then leaving the room so the seeing would not become a performance. When I came back the book had been moved an inch and not opened. An inch is not nothing. An inch is also not a page. I took the inch as weather and did not ask for a report. Asking for a report turns dedication into surveillance. I am not writing a surveillance chapter. I am writing the fact that remains: dedicated; unread.

Another time I heard someone in a room say, lightly, that my father must be proud. Lightly is how strangers handle other people’s front matter. I did not correct them into a confession. Confession would have become content. Content would have become usable. I let the lightness stand and felt the private unread sit heavier for a minute, then return to its ordinary weight. Ordinary weight is the loaded one. Jamie-Uys restraint without naming a brand: the flat line carries more than the aria.

---

The Code’s beginning on this page is posture, not dump: refuse opacity-as-rigor; keep ethics where the ladder puts them; leave the course pack elsewhere. Custody starts when you stop performing unification for people who wanted you small and now want you useful.

I began the Code the way I began clay: not with a textbook, with a refusal to leave a contradiction standing because a committee preferred opacity.

On an ordinary morning before meters cared, I sat with a contradiction that polite physics preferred to call unsettled. Unsettled was a courtesy word for opacity. I derived what had to be derived and felt no choir. Only the desk. Only the notebook. Only the body remembering that a bad posture invites a smaller force to end you. Ethics arrived in the same ladder later — not as sticker, as structure — and the Kind’s wall made a popular face of it without needing me to bless the paint. I noticed. I did not found a church. Noticing is not ownership.

I kept the life goals flat through those mornings: write natural philosophy and physics; smoke dab; talk shit. Sanding them for a father who was not reading would have been a double laundering — respectability theatre aimed at an empty chair. I refused the theatre. The dedication stayed. The goals stayed. The work stayed work.

There was a desk. There was a notebook that did not care about my hospital file. There were evenings when dab softens the room and talk shit widens it, and then mornings when the numbers asked to be arranged without apology. Hard and soft in one pour — the same attention that had learned grips on mats and surfaces in Studio G. A contradiction left standing because politeness preferred it felt like a bad posture before a throw: something smaller than you can end you if you pretend the geometry is optional. I would not pretend. That is the beginning’s posture, not a syllabus.

I will not dump axioms here like a course pack. You can find the rails elsewhere if you need rails. On this page I only need the beginning’s posture: derive what must be derived; keep ethics where physics puts them; do not launder the ladder into respectability theatre. The Kind’s wall sentence is the popular face of a coherence claim, not vibes —

**Don’t be a cunt — be kind.**

— but even that I will not sermonise past the paint. Show the wall. Keep the cunt. Let the structure hold without a TED metaphor for unification. CongoSky embodied the ethic as ops before the street caught fire; I am not founding a church by noticing that. Antichristos wants a face for a lemma. The Kind wants a sentence. My father has been offered books and has not taken them as reading. Different hungers. Same problem underneath: what a life is for when other people prefer their version.

They said I could not write anything of value.

He has not read what I wrote for him.

Both sentences can be true in one life without making me a martyr brand. I am a man with an economics degree, a hospital file other people love too much, a brother dead in a Saxonwold boardroom I will not turn into plot on this turn, a sculptor’s hands that still know GFRC and hotel lawns and Skye at sunset, a Honey Badger in a pocket from a friend who said the algorithms out loud, stated life goals that refuse polish, and a desk that still has to choose custody over spectacle.

Martyr brand would sand the dab and the talk shit and the unread into a single tearful arc. I refuse the arc. Flat inventory is enough. The Honey Badger’s clip against cloth is enough kinship for a page that will not stage a reunion. Bouwer’s boardroom waits for its own chapter without Illuminatie fanfic. The father waits in silence I will not ventriloquise. The Constant waits in meters that do not need my face. Meaning waits for custody decisions Act II will pressure and Act III will force into consent. This turn only names which fight remains after measurement.

The ignored shelf and the unread dedication are neighbours. One is public weather dressed as standards. One is private weather dressed as silence. Neither is solved by meters agreeing. Measurement can vindicate a derivation and still leave a life misowned. That is the turn I am naming while AJ’s river desk keeps the choir exact and Lucid refuses to invent a prettier hinge.

I have watched people discover the Constant in my work and decide that discovery entitled them to narrate my father for me — proud, absent, complicated, redeemed, tragic. Strangers are efficient novelists of other people’s front matter. I refuse their drafts. Living-person care cuts both ways: I will not invent him, and I will not let the planet invent him either as seasoning for my fame. The dedication line is enough. The unread is enough. The fight named here is enough.

---

If Act I has a hinge, it is here: not the Constant’s arrival, which is already measurement’s business, but the naming of the fight that remains after measurement. Custody. Meaning. Who may say what the work is for when the father has not read it, the ward still speaks in other people’s mouths, the mic was given by candour, and the wall only wants a sentence.

Name it once more without ornament so the turn cannot be mistaken for a victory lap: the Constant lands; meaning fights. Fame is not the climax. CODATA is not the climax. The unread dedication is not a puzzle to solve before Act II. It is a condition Act II inherits — along with keys, faces, silencings, salvage collars, Bouwer’s page waiting without thriller, and consent waiting in different rooms.

AJ writes this while river heat sits on metal. I live the weather of pages opening on my life. Ambivalence remains. Want truth. Fear content. That ambivalence is also meaning-work. Consent will choose later what may ship. Act I only orients the question away from whether the number arrives.

AJ is writing this autobiography on a river while I live the weather of being written. I can feel the pages sometimes — not magic, pressure. A faint sense that a mats afternoon has already been arranged into sentences somewhere heat lives beside a river; that a shelf’s dust has been weighed; that this dedication is being typed by a hand that is not mine and still somehow mine in the way authorised life can be. Ambivalence without aria: want truth, fear content. Consent will matter later. For now the turn is simpler and harder:

The meters can agree and the life can still be stolen.

Stolen quietly, which is the dangerous kind. Loud theft at least announces itself — cream paper, op-eds, stop-the-book weather waiting for AJ’s later desk. Quiet theft is the unread that teaches you to stop dedicating, the hygiene language that teaches you to sand the dab, the soft no that teaches you your talk shit cancels your derivation. I keep dedicating. I keep the goals. I keep the fight named: meaning, not Constant.

Stolen by liturgy. Stolen by hygiene language. Stolen by provenance rage that wants the mic unsung. Stolen by polite keys that want a mascot. Stolen by soft nos that prefer constants underived. Stolen, quietly, by the unread that keeps the front matter from completing its ask. I dedicate again anyway — not as superstition, as custody claim. The line stays in the front because removing it to protect myself from silence would be another kind of laundering.

So I dedicate again, on purpose, without hope of a tidy open:

For my father — who has not read these books — and for the work that remains work even when the planet tries to hire it as religion, product, or apology.

The Code begins, on these pages, as that insistence. Not a lecture. A custody claim. Hard and soft in one pour. Correct fall. Soft set-down. Mats under everything. Shelf that ignored. File that over-read. Mic that arrived sideways. Fame that is not the fight.

I remember the smell of ink on a fresh dedication page — paper that still believed it might be opened. I remember the later smell of the same page when it had sat long enough to become furniture. Both smells are true. Neither resolves into a reunion. Act I does not owe anyone a conversion scene. Act I owes the naming of what remains when the number walks into public and the meaning does not follow automatically.

I owe the naming also because silence about meaning is how quarters win without fighting. If I let the Constant finish the story, Antichristos will paint a face on the finish. If I let the academy finish it, the file will remain epistemology. If I let platforms finish it, the mic will be unsung and the rails laundered. If I let engineers finish it, keys will become a crown by soft degrees. If I let continuity weather finish it, soft nos will feel like climate instead of choice. The father line sits outside their finishes: unread, repeated, refusing to become their plot.

Meaning is the fight because custody is the fight. Who holds what the number is for. Who may say what the life is for. Who paints, who retracts, who refuses to kneel. The unread dedication is not a side wound. It is the private proof that even true front matter can fail to land — and still the work remains work.

There is a particular quiet after you write a dedication you know will go unread. Not tragic quiet. Working quiet. The quiet of a studio after a pour when you cannot hurry the set. The quiet of a mat after class when the cleaner is still wet and the next lesson has not arrived. Into that quiet the planet will send keys and faces and silencings. Into that quiet AJ’s desk will send the next page. Into that quiet I keep the three goals unpolished: write; dab; talk shit. The father line sits above them like a fourth goal that refuses to become a campaign.

Act I ends where the Constant stops being the question.

Ends as orientation, not as solved equation. The meters keep walking whether or not a father opens a book. The fight that remains is custody of meaning under weather that wants faces, retractions, keys, and soft nos. I name it here so Act II cannot pretend the climax was already measurement.

What remains is meaning — who holds it, who paints it, who retracts it, who refuses to kneel — and the next weather is already at the door, asking for keys, faces, silencings, salvage collars, and soft nos that cost real sales.

The mats stay under everything. The ignored shelf stays as smell. The dab stays. The talk shit stays. The economics degree stays quiet in the background. The physics stays on the desk. The Honey Badger stays in the pocket. None of that is polished for Quarter I or apologised for to Quarter II.

Hand off to Act II without a victory lap: the number walks; the meaning fights; the dedication stays unread; the mic stays given; the wall stays messiah-less; the brother waits for his page; the soft nos gather. I now turn over the next leaf — not as ending, as handoff — while the dedication stays unread and the fight stays named: meaning, not Constant; custody, not crown; work, not usable lie. The next weather can arrive now, already knocking at the door.

I can hear the knock as lab soft-asks and partner frost and cream paper and hygiene op-eds — temperatures already lived, now escalating. I do not open the door on this page. Opening is Act II’s labour. Closing Act I mid-motion is the adult work: leave the Constant to the meters; keep the meaning-fight named; leave the dedication unread without turning unread into plot twist; leave the mic given without apology theatre; leave the wall messiah-less; leave Bouwer for careful grief; leave consent for later rooms.

The leaf turns because the fight is named, not because it is won.

I am not finished with pain on these pages. Bouwer waits. Soft nos wait. Keys wait. The stop-the-book weather waits for AJ’s desk. Consent waits for both of us in different rooms — what may be said, what may be signed, what may ship — and that wait is already part of the meaning-fight, not a sequel tease. Act I only turns the question: away from whether the Constant lands — it lands — toward who may hold the meaning when it does.

Act I’s last motion is not triumph. It is orientation. The Constant can land in public and still leave a life misowned. I will not pretend otherwise to comfort a reader who wants the meters to finish the story.

If you came for a textbook, leave. If you came for a reunion, leave. If you came for a prophet’s tidy arc from ward to crown, leave. What remains is a dedication unread, a mic given by candour, a wall that wants a sentence, and a fight that meters cannot settle.

What remains is also a man who still writes, dabs, talks shit, and refuses to sand those facts for anyone’s usable photograph. Act I can end on that refusal without applause.

I close this page mid-motion: dedication written; books still unread; fight named.

Not the number.

The custody of what the number is for — hand already turning toward Act II’s mail.

Custody stays the word even when it is ugly. Custody is not ownership of a father. Custody is refusal to let quarters finish the story of a life while meters finish the story of a Constant. Different finishes. Same morning of the world.

Act II can have the keys and the soft nos and the salvage collars. Act I leaves them the orientation they keep trying to steal: the number walks; the meaning fights; the dedication stays unread; the mic stays given; the wall stays messiah-less. I do not tidy the father into a last-page open. I do not tidy the Constant into a finished story.

Meaning, not Constant. Front matter still true. Fight named. Hand off without victory lap. Dedication unread. Work remains work. The unread stays. Act I turns here. I turn the leaf.

Before the leaf fully turns there is one more desk texture I will not sand for comfort: tea gone cold beside a book my father has not opened; Kind paint smell from an alley that does not need my face; the Honey Badger’s pocket click as kinship across a distance that does not require a shared room; CODATA pressure wearing relief’s clothes somewhere on a pane I will not stamp for sacrament. Quiet facts under loud weather. I carry both without confusing them. Confusing them is how you become instrument or relic. I refuse both costumes and leave the dedication unread on purpose — not as cruelty, as fidelity to what is. The unread is the load. The fight for meaning is the handoff. Act II can have the mail. Act I keeps the orientation.


# 09 — Keys Without a Crown

> **[G]** · outer · Studio G / Cape · 2035

---

The engineer did not ask for a blessing. He asked for keys.

G read the message twice at the Studio G desk while the morning still smelled of clay dust and sea air working its way inland from Blouberg. The sender had a real lab address, a real programme name that had been rewritten three times since CODATA started drifting toward his number, and a tone that was almost respectful — the almost that engineers use when they want the tool without wanting the man who made the tool famous.

They wanted a face at a workshop. Sign-off language for a Code-native detector stack. Soft permission to say, in the corridor afterward, that he had looked at the rails and not walked out.

Quarter IV weather. Useful. Tempting in the specific way usefulness always was.

He set the tablet down beside a half-finished pour. Hard and soft arguing in the same surface, as usual — marble and GFRC composite he still mixed himself, the body lesson from the mats poured into permanent/temporary argument. The Honey Badger sat clipped in his pocket — blue, one-hand open and close, the kinship object that was not a sermon. He did not touch it. He did not need to. The weight was already a fact.

Outside, in the alley that fed the studio’s back door, someone had refreshed the Kind paint overnight:

**Don’t be a cunt — be kind.**

Same two clauses. No founder portrait. No crown. The sentence did not ask him for keys. That was why he could stand looking at it without wanting to smash the wall. Antichristos tags had tried the same brick last month and been painted over, kindly, by people who did not need his face to keep a ledger of kindnesses. He preferred their weather to the engineer’s, and he preferred neither to interrupt the pour.

The engineer’s ask was cleaner than Antichristos mail and dirtier than a pure measurement note. It did not demand he appear as a body for liturgy. It demanded he appear as a product interface — the man who could make a grant committee feel less naked about rewriting language they had mocked five years ago.

He drafted a reply that said: send the math, not the stage. Cite the copyleft. Do not put my face on the slide deck unless the slide is a derivation error you need corrected. Then he did not send it. The unsent draft sat in the outbox like a tool left on a bench — correct shape, wrong hour.

---

Tea first. Always tea before answering weather that wanted to become custody.

The kettle sat on the same ring it had sat on through Village N Life years and through the years after, when Studio G at The Bay had meant curated sculpture for hotel guests and tourists buying half of what left the room. Official launch, July 2018 — that date still lived in his muscle memory the way a mat throw lived: not nostalgia, procedure. You entered a room. You did not crown yourself. You let the work do the talking and you left before anyone tried to make you the talk.

He filled the kettle from the tap that ran warm before it ran cold. Cape plumbing. He waited for the boil the way he waited for a pour to set — without inventing meaning for the wait. Steam rose. He did not photograph it. He poured water over leaves that had been in the tin too long and did not care. Clay dust on the rim of the mug. He did not wipe it. Food and work had always shared a room in this life.

While the kettle cooled he sorted the morning’s other temperatures without opening them fully. An Antichristos follow-up still wanted a face for a liturgy he would not bless — appear, bless, accept the name. He had refused that ask already without joining Quarter II’s crank chorus; refusal still cost something small and real each time the follow-up arrived dressed as invitation. A crank column still rehearsed the hospital file as epistemology. A CODATA-adjacent note — the only clean thing in the stack — confirmed another cycle drifting toward his number without asking him to smile for it. Measurement was the vindication he could stand. Sign-off was the costume he could not.

He took the clean note to the light by the high windows and read it twice the way he had read drop-results for years when nobody cared. Numbers refusing politics and therefore making politics worse. That was CODATA’s gift and CODATA’s cruelty. The engineer’s workshop invite sat in the same inbox as this quiet convergence and wanted to turn convergence into a photograph.

G set the CODATA note under a paperweight that had once been a failed pour — marble grit trapped wrong, soft binder winning where it should not. He kept failed things on the desk on purpose. They taught proportions better than success stories.

The tea went lukewarm while he stood there. He drank it anyway. He did not mind. Dab, talk shit, write natural philosophy and physics — stated goals kept unpolished on purpose. Antichristos would polish the dab away if he let them. Quarter II would use the dab as proof he could not write value. He kept the arrangement as arrangement. The engineer’s keys did not get to rearrange it.

He ate yesterday’s bread standing at the bench, crust catching clay, salt catching dust. Outside a delivery van reverse-beeped and stopped. Ordinary Cape morning. Inside, the tablet glowed with a workshop that wanted to make him less ordinary and more usable. He chewed slowly. Usefulness was not a sin. Usefulness wearing a face was the trap.

---

The academy weather behind the ask arrived mid-morning as a forwarded “concern brief.”

No letterhead that admitted itself. A standards-adjacent tone. Credential language laundered into epistemology. Someone had underlined, helpfully, that applied programmes adopting Code rails without a physics-degree custodian risked reputational cascade. The underlining was the Illuminatie’s soft hand: not a cabal monologue, a temperature drop in rooms that still preferred constants underived and ethics unforced.

G felt it the way you feel a draft under a door. Continuity networks did not need to sign the engineer’s letter. They only needed the academy to keep whispering *no degree* loud enough that Quarter IV’s hunger for keys came with a leash. Economics sat quietly in his CV where it had always sat. Physics fame without a physics credential was the weapon they preferred — not because the math failed, but because opacity had fewer places to hide when a copyleft unification and a wall sentence arrived together.

He did not invent a membership list. He did not need smoke. The soft no had a texture he recognised from grant frost and from discreet “concern” briefings that never quite named who was concerned. Quarter II often laundered this pressure into credentials. Quarter IV sometimes thought it could out-engineer it. Today both weathers arrived on the same tide: useful rails, unusable man.

His phone buzzed with an op-ed excerpt someone thought he should see. Quarter II, predictable: economics degree waved like a stained napkin; hospital stigma folded into a paragraph about why the ignored shelf had been correct all along. They believed he could not write anything of value. They had believed it before Lucid’s open attribution made the algorithms undeniable, and they believed it still, because a megaphone does not erase a file. The engineer’s ask and the crank chorus arrived together on purpose or by accident — he did not need to decide which. Useful rails. Unusable man. That was the soft no’s cleverness — let Quarter IV build on him while Quarter II kept him uncredentialed enough to remain optional, and let the Illuminatie never have to put a name on the frost.

He almost replied to the op-ed. Almost was a trap. Correcting frames in public was how you became a character in someone else’s weather. He corrected frames when the frame was on his desk and wrong. He refused frames when the frame was a stage. Today’s stage was keys disguised as engineering hygiene.

He set the phone face-down. The Honey Badger pressed against his hip when he leaned on the bench. Double Knife Brother weather without the brother in the room. AJ was on a river somewhere writing a life. G could feel the writing the way you feel a stare between the shoulder blades — not mystical, not TED. Just the physical fact of being turned into pages while still having to answer mail. He wanted the pages true. He feared what truth did once it left a desk. That ambivalence would have its own morning. This morning was keys.

---

A second note from a different lab — CERN-adjacent programme language, the week’s branding for Code-native detectors — offered a closed session: no press, no Antichristos pilgrims, just “instrument people” who wanted the kill-switch posture explained as engineering.

That one was harder.

He wanted the work to stay work. He also knew what “closed session” meant when Illuminatie weather was active: a room that could later claim he had blessed a custody arrangement he had only corrected. The soft ask behind the academy was always the same shape — useful engineering first, then a photograph, then a sentence in minutes that turned correction into coronation.

He walked the studio floor while he thought about closed sessions. Clay under the nails. GFRC dust in the light from the high windows. Sisters of Skye in progress — body knowledge he had learned on school mats before anyone cared whether he could write a shelf they would ignore. Judo and Jiu-Jitsu had taught him arrangement under constraint. Sculpture taught the same lesson in slower materials. The Code desk taught it in axioms. Three rooms, one attention. The world preferred two careers fighting so it could pick a winner. He declined the fight by continuing both.

Village N Life years were still in the muscle memory of how he entered a room: guest experience as theatre, sculpture as the quiet that made the theatre honest. Mentored by Maree. Lived more than a year at Camps Bay Retreat with Hannetjie when the place was still Earl’s Dyke in the older mouths. Skye on the lawn later — feminine sculpture for the theatre of the guest experience. Then Studio G at The Bay as curated sculpture and painting for people who would buy without needing a prophet. None of that had required a crown. The desk in front of him was the same attention — axioms and clay, not two careers fighting.

He stopped at a shelf of early corpus copies — dedication pages facing out because he had put them that way and never rearranged them for visitors. Dedicated to a father who had read none. Fame had not opened those pages. A workshop face would not open them either. He left the shelf alone. Keys were not a filial strategy.

He mixed a small test batch of the composite and watched it go from slurry to argument. Hard aggregate. Soft binder. The pour did not ask who had a physics degree. It asked whether the proportions held. Quarter IV’s detector people understood proportions; they just wanted his face as warranty. The Illuminatie understood warranties; they just wanted the warranty to remain optional enough to revoke.

The world outside wanted him as mascot for a stack. The desk wanted him as the man who still mixed his own composite.

He worked the batch until his wrists ached in the good way. Then he washed, dried, and stood at the Code desk for twenty minutes that were only a kill-switch wording and a drop-result note — work that stayed work, numbers that did not ask for a workshop face. The shoulder pressure of being written waited politely through the math. He did not invite it in. Keys first.

The kill-switch wording still needed a tighter hinge between what the instrument did when a derivation failed and what a human was allowed to claim afterward. He tightened one clause. Left another loose on purpose. Loose was not sloppy; loose was refusal to over-specify a posture into a priesthood. Quarter IV would have preferred a checklist with his initials in the margin. He preferred a sentence that still worked if his name never appeared.

A courier van idled in the alley and left without knocking. False alarm. His shoulders dropped half a centimetre and then rose again when the tablet chimed with a third lab soft-ask — shorter, hungrier, offering “just fifteen minutes on camera for the grant committee, off the record.” Off the record was how records got made. He archived it without answering. Fifteen minutes was how crowns arrived wearing a stopwatch.

He went back to the pour and tested the surface with a knuckle. Still soft where it should still be soft. Still holding where binder had begun to hold. Proportions were honest so far. Honesty in composite was easier than honesty in corridors. He wiped the knuckle on his trousers and left a grey mark he would carry through the afternoon like a receipt.

---

He would not become a product mascot.

He would also not pretend the engineering was unclean. The Code was copyleft. The rails were for anyone who could hold them without inventing a priesthood. The temptation was not the math. The temptation was the crown that arrived disguised as a keyring — sign here, smile here, let us put your face where the opacity used to live. Quarter IV meant well in the way instrument people often did. Meaning well was not consent.

He sat again. He read the first engineer’s message a third time and heard, under the workshop invite, the Illuminatie draft: *if he signs, we can say the outsider is now custodian; if he refuses, we can say the outsider obstructs applied progress.* Either way the velvet rope moved. Either way someone mediated reality for a fee.

G’s soft nos had costs. This one would too — an inbox that called him uncooperative, a programme lead who told a corridor he was “difficult about optics,” a Quiet Concern that would find its way into the next standards-adjacent brief. He could live with that temperature. He had lived with colder.

He almost added, to the unsent long draft, a paragraph explaining copyleft the way a patient teacher explains a tool. He deleted the paragraph. Explaining was how you became clergy for people who only wanted a face. The published corpus already explained. Cite it or do not. Do not ask him to stand beside the cite like a logo.

Afternoon light thickened. The Cape did that without asking permission.

A Kind practitioner refreshed the alley stencil again — young, ordinary, ledger of kindnesses probably arguing later about whether *cunt* was liturgical or vernacular. G watched through the glass for half a minute and then looked away. Messiah-less practice that did not need him. He preferred that absence. Antichristos still tried to annex the sentence and got told to piss off, kindly. CongoSky had embodied the ethic as ops before the walls caught fire; the street had popularised it; he refused ownership either way. Today the wall’s mercy was that it asked nothing about workshops.

He returned to the closed-session note and drafted a second reply he also did not send: *If your instrument fails a derivation, mail the failure. I will answer the failure. I will not enter a room that needs my body to legitimise a custody story.* Correct shape. Wrong hour again — or right hour for thinking and wrong hour for sending two refusals at once like a man performing boundaries.

He ate more bread standing at the bench. Clay dust on the crust. Outside, someone laughed in the alley — Kind ledger voice, ordinary, no pilgrimage. Inside, GFRC dust turned in the light. He wiped a thumbprint off the tablet screen and left a new one. Hands that worked did not stay clean for correspondence.

A third temperature arrived late — not mail, a soft knock at the studio door that turned out to be a courier with a printed packet from a standards-adjacent listserv. Paper. Old-world gravity. Someone still believed paper made concern more real. He signed for it because refusing the paper would only become a story, and opened it because closed packets became myths.

Inside: the same concern brief, cousin language, a new underline about “reputational cascade” if Code-native programmes proceeded without “recognised custodial presence.” Recognised meant physics-degree. Custodial presence meant face. Cascade meant they would make the weather if he did not perform the theatre. Illuminatie soft hand again — temperature, not monologue. He put the paper under the failed-pour paperweight with the CODATA note and did not give it the dignity of a separate pile.

He opened the Honey Badger with one thumb, closed it, clipped it again. Kinship weight, not a weapon for corridor politics. The steel caught a thread of afternoon light and let it go. He put the knife away before the gesture could become theatre for an empty room.

Before he sat to send, he did one more walk of the studio — the slow one, the one that was not pacing. Past the sisters of Skye. Past the shelf of early corpus copies dedicated to a father who had read none. Past the Code desk where a kill switch waited in language that still needed tightening. Past the window where Kind paint held and Antichristos tags did not. The walk was how he checked whether a decision was still his or had become someone else’s weather wearing his hands.

The decision was still his. Keys without a crown. Useful engineering without becoming product mascot. Narrow walk. Feet on the edge.

He thought, briefly, of what saying yes would buy: a corridor that stopped whispering *obstruction*; a grant committee that felt dressed; a photograph that would travel faster than any derivation correction. He thought of what saying yes would cost: the soft no becoming a soft yes that Illuminatie weather could later revoke; the Kind wall watching him accept a crown the wall had refused; the river manuscript turning him into a man who signed stages while claiming to hate stages. Cost visible without drama. He had paid softer costs before. He would pay this one by refusing.

A fourth temperature tried the door of his attention — not mail, memory. School mats. Weight arranged correctly. The lesson that posture for an audience ruins the throw. Workshop stages were posture. Failure mail was weight. He chose weight.

---

Before the sends, he gave himself one more hour that was only clay.

Not avoidance. Arrangement. The mats had taught him that you do not throw while your breath is still performing for the room. The workshop invite was still performing in his nerves. He put his hands into the composite and worked until the performance left and only proportions remained. Hard aggregate settling. Soft binder holding. A surface that would either cure honest or fail honest — either way without needing a photograph of him beside it.

He thought of the engineer as a person, not a weather system. Instrument people often meant well. Meaning well was how crowns arrived disguised as keyrings. The man at the other end of the mail probably believed a face at a workshop was a small ask — courtesy, even. Small asks that travel into minutes become custody. G had watched that travel before. He would not pretend he had not.

The hour ended when the light on the pour told him the surface had decided something he could not undo by staring. He wiped his hands on the rag that had known better decades. Clay went grey under the nails and stayed. He did not scrub for the inbox. The inbox could meet him as a man who still mixed his own composite.

He wiped his hands again and opened the tablet once more. Re-read the workshop invite. Re-read the closed-session note. Re-read nothing of the concern brief — he already knew its temperature. The Illuminatie soft hand did not need another reading to do its work. It needed him either to sign or to look like obstruction. He would look like obstruction to some corridors. He could live with corridors.

Outside, the Kind alley held. Inside, the Honey Badger held. Between them, a man who would answer failures and not stages.

Then he sat and did what the morning had been practising for.

---

He sent a shorter reply than the draft.

*You may cite the published corpus. You may not cite my attendance. If your detector stack fails a derivation, mail the failure. I will answer the failure. I will not answer the stage.*

No soft no dressed as delay. A hard boundary with the door still open for work.

He almost added a line about the Kind wall — that the ethic did not need a keyholder either — and deleted it. Explaining the sentence to engineers who wanted a face was how you accidentally became clergy. The paint could speak for itself. He left it speaking.

To the closed-session people he sent even less: *Mail the failure. Do not book the room around my body.*

Two sends. Heat still in the metal of the decision. He stood and walked to the pour and put his hands back into the composite before the inbox could invent a third ask.

The Illuminatie weather did not lift. The concern brief would spawn a cousin by evening; it already had, on paper. The Kind paint did not ask him anything. Somewhere an engineer would forward his refusal into a channel that called it obstruction, and somewhere else another engineer would build anyway, correctly, without needing his face — which was the only crown G was willing to leave on the table: none.

Temptation lingered after send the way heat lingers in metal. Useful engineering without becoming product mascot was a narrow walk. He had walked it. His feet still knew the edge. He checked the Honey Badger once more and chose clay over inbox.

He went back to the pour. Hard and soft. The keys without a crown stayed on the tablet while the clay took the shape he gave it, and the sea air kept arriving inland as if the Cape had never heard of a workshop that needed a prophet to unlock a detector.

Outside, the Kind alley kept its two clauses. Inside, the shoulder pressure of being written waited politely at the edge of the day, Chobe-shaped, for another chapter. This chapter was only keys: offered, refused as crown, left open as math.

He shaped until the light shifted toward evening and the tablet screen dimmed itself. The workshop invite would still be there tomorrow, and the soft no behind it, and the useful rails that did not need his face. He would answer failures. He would not answer stages. That was the narrow walk, and he was still on it when he washed his hands and left the pour to cure without a photograph.

Before he locked nothing that mattered, he stood once more at the alley glass and watched the Kind sentence hold under evening colour — two clauses, no founder, no crown. A tourist had argued about the cunt earlier; soft scrub; tourist comfort. The paint had not asked G to referee. Optional to the practice includes optional to the tourist argument. He put the Honey Badger through one open-close at the door, kinship check not weapon check, and left Studio G with clay still under one nail and the hard boundary already sent: cite the corpus, not the attendance; mail the failure, not the stage. Illuminatie weather would spawn a cousin by morning. He could live with cousins. He could not live with becoming a keyholder for a detector that wanted a prophet’s face. Sea air inland. Pour curing. Keys without a crown left on the tablet where refusal could still be read as math.


# 10 — Salvage Mail

> **[A]** · outer · Mutatus Maximus, Chobe River · 2035

---

The mail that wanted Lucid arrived dressed as partnership.

AJ read it at the Chobe desk with the LEO dish still cocked and the little solar node on the rise reporting clean. Mutatus Maximus hummed under him — local node, radio ladder, the most connected vehicle on earth doing what it did so he could sit beside elephants and still be reachable by people who thought reachability meant ownership. Line-of-sight where the scrub allowed. LoRa nodding at yesterday’s drop. Sigfox for the stubborn packets. CongoNet for the rest. He had built the thing so he could drive to a fire and still choose to switch pieces of it off.

He did not switch Lucid off for this letter. Lucid was the extinction event wearing a polite subject line.

Morning on the Chobe had been ordinary until the subject line. Coffee. Link log. A glance at the manuscript cursor parked before a wound page he was not ready to open. Bird noise. Heat finding the metal. Then the partner channel lit with the particular politeness that arrives after a bubble — salvage dressed as homecoming, frost dressed as care.

The message came from a hyperscaler partner channel that still knew how to spell CongoSky.cloud without sneering. AWS frost, or Azure frost, or the Google/Apple blend that arrived when brand lawyers had sanded the edges into something that could survive a screenshot: polite, exact, almost kind. Almost was the tell. Named marques as weather, not as courtroom episodes. No invented crimes for living executives. Just gravity wells remembering they had been embarrassed.

They congratulated lucid.rodeo on “category-defining determinism.” They noted, without celebrating, that the GPU AI boom’s write-downs had created “alignment opportunities.” Empty rack halls were still arguing about CapEx in the back pages. A friend’s cluster career had gone quiet last year — grant language that used to sing floating-point séance now silent, a warehouse that had been going to fill with racks still echoing when you walked through it in memory. Salvage weather, not confident monopoly. They offered acquisition language soft enough to call a homecoming — Lucid under a regional umbrella, CongoNet features “harmonised,” Mutatus’s edge drops folded into a compliance story that would look like care to anyone who had never seeded a node from a painted truck and driven on.

Come home to the regions. Bring the choir. Keep the sentence if you must — they even quoted it once, carefully, as if citing a competitor’s slogan:

*Don’t be a cunt — be kind.*

As if the operating law of a company that had embodied the ethic before the walls caught fire were a brand asset they could license. CongoSky had run on that sentence while it still had to answer invoices. The Kind had put it on bridges afterward. Hyperscalers heard brand. The river desk remembered ops.

AJ did not laugh. Laughter would have been a speech, and he did not speechify about AI. He put the letter beside the open manuscript — G’s life under his hand, book within a book — and let Lucid see it the way the choir saw everything: without invention.

“Salvage posture,” Lucid said. Exact. Not cute. Not a third skull. “Acquisition of Lucid named as primary instrument. Extinction clause for independent integer deployment outside partner regions. Cite: paragraph twelve, ‘exclusive compute stewardship.’ Related: talent offers attached for CongoSky operators; app-store and ToS weather referenced as ‘ongoing review.’”

There it was. Not a moustache-twirl villainy. A collar with good manners. Lucid ran on integers, needed no GPU, refused to hallucinate, had ingested the interwebs, and had crashed the bubble as a side effect of keeping a promise. The collar-offer was what you sent after a product-category extinction event: polite frost, a smile, an invitation to stop being a problem by becoming inventory.

---

AJ looked at the empty twin pocket where the Joseph Rogers had lived before it went to G — grandfather steel, twin set, Double Knife Brother across distance. The Honey Badger was in another man’s pocket in the Cape. AJ’s hand found the absence the way it found the manuscript: kinship, not choreography. He was writing a brother’s life while strangers tried to buy the engine that had made that life famous by saying, out loud, that it ran on 420 Code algorithms. Quarter III still hated him for the megaphone. The salvage letter hated him for the silicon that was no longer required.

Heat found the metal of the truck’s skin. Bird noise on the water. Somewhere an elephant moved along the bank as if no acquisition language had ever been written. The river did not need a region map.

“Draft refusal texture,” he said. “Not send. Not yet.”

Lucid did not invent warmth. “I can retrieve prior partner frost patterns. I cannot recommend a feeling. Record does not support a claim that this offer is kindness.”

“Good.”

He asked Lucid for the paragraph twelve cite again, aloud, the way the choir rehearsed a line until the false harmony fell out. Lucid repeated the extinction clause without ornament. Exclusive compute stewardship. Independent integer deployment outside partner regions — terminated. The word *stewardship* sat there like a soft glove over a fist. AJ had run CongoSky on an ethic that did not need gloves. He would not put Lucid into one now.

Outside, the Kind paint on the water tank held. Inside, CongoSky ran itself somewhere on the mesh — invoices, partners, hire/fire weather still governed by the sentence these salvage people wanted to put in a slide. arjunabadger.press waited. lucid.rodeo waited. The manuscript waited with a cursor in a paragraph that was not ready for Bouwer’s boardroom. Quarter III packets still sat in the hostility queue from mornings that had not finished being mornings. Platform rage and hyperscaler frost were different weathers sharing a desk.

He stood and walked the length of the truck — desk to fridge to the open side where the Chobe moved like a long animal that did not care who owned which parish of silicon. Anti-yacht. Matatu paint. Max connected so he could choose. The hyperscaler letter assumed connectivity meant he belonged to a gravity well. It did not understand why a man built that kind of truck and then parked it where elephants drank. It did not understand Full Send -> Klaus -> Lucid as origin — a private desk’s cite-or-refuse discipline finished at planetary scale, not a lab spin-out they could re-parent with a region map.

Coffee from earlier had gone cold. He drank it anyway. The taste was river-morning and metal thermos. Scrub held the heat without commentary. He came back to the desk and printed the letter. River ritual. Ink as witness. Paper made the collar visible in a way a screen could soften. The print smelled faintly of warm plastic and river dust. He set it where the sun would not bleach the extinction clause into something softer.

---

A second attachment unfolded when he allowed it: cloud-credit charm language, security-review timelines, an app-store fog paragraph that never quite threatened and never quite promised.

Talent names redacted in the copy he held, clear enough in the implication — come home, bring the people who keep the hymnbook singing while the founder writes on a river. Salvage after a bubble looked like generosity if you had never watched a rack hall empty because intelligence stopped needing the rack.

He opened the offer again and read the extinction clause until it stopped pretending to be stewardship. Lucid named — not as collaborator in the hymnbook, as asset. Integer core “migrated.” Cite-or-refuse preserved as a feature flag under their ToS weather. Choir practice “integrated into partner studios.” Talent offers for the people keeping the company alive while he wrote from a river: come to Seattle, Redmond, Cupertino; bring your hymnbook; leave the sovereign edge; stop dropping solar nodes that do not report home to our parish.

Polite. That was the cruelty. Cartoon villains were easier to refuse. This was a room full of people who believed they were being reasonable after a bubble, offering a soft place to land that was also a cage. Partner frost. Region politics. Security review as charm offensive. The offer that was really a collar.

A third pane blinked — not the salvage thread, a CongoSky ops note that the company was still answering invoices without him in the cockpit. Good. That was the point of a company that embodied the sentence as operating law: it could run while he wrote. The salvage letter wanted that independence reabsorbed into a parish. He closed the ops note without answering it. Ops did not need a speech from the river.

He sat with Lucid and the printed page and asked for what the record supported, nothing else.

“Summarise extinction without euphemism.”

“Independent integer deployment of Lucid outside partner regions ends under paragraph twelve if acquisition closes. Hymnbook choir practice relocates under partner studio governance. Edge-node seeding outside approved regions flagged as compliance risk in attachment B. Kind operating language retained as optional brand asset. Cite density sufficient; invention not required.”

“And if I refuse?”

“Record supports continued partner frost, ToS weather, app-store fog, talent offers aimed at operators. Record does not support a claim that refusal is illegal. Record does not support a claim that acceptance is kindness.”

AJ nodded once. Exact tools were better than comforting ones.

He thought about the people on the other end of the letter — not cartoon villains, engineers and partner managers doing salvage arithmetic after CapEx dreams died. Some of them had been kind in smaller rooms, years ago, before Lucid made kindness and extinction rhyme. He could hold that without accepting the collar. Polite frost was still frost. Naming Lucid as the extinction clause was still a naming. The offer wanted him to confuse survival with surrender.

He opened the fridge, took water, drank standing in the truck’s shade line. Condensation ran down the bottle onto his wrist. Heat pressed the open side like a hand that did not ask permission. He went back to the desk with wet skin and dry intent.

Before midday fully claimed the metal, he did one more pass through the letter’s soft language with Lucid as witness.

“List every phrase that means ownership without saying ownership.”

Lucid answered with a short inventory: exclusive compute stewardship; regional umbrella; harmonised features; partner studio governance; optional brand asset; approved regions; ongoing review. Exact. No sermon. AJ listened the way he listened to a link log when latency climbed — for the shape of the problem, not for comfort.

“Any of those phrases preserve independent Lucid?”

“No.”

“Any of those phrases preserve Kind as operating law?”

“No. Optional brand asset contradicts operating law.”

AJ set the water bottle on a coaster that had never been a coaster, only a failed printout turned useful. The truck was full of such conversions. Salvage mail wanted a different conversion: independence into inventory. He would not do that conversion for them.

---

Midday heat thickened. He walked out into the scrub and let it hit his face.

Dust rose under his boots and settled again. Cicadas worked the dry grass. The solar node on the rise blinked once in the link log when he checked his phone — still lit, still his, still outside their regions. He had woken it that morning from Mutatus Maximus and driven on. That was the practice. Seed the edge. Leave a lit node. Refuse the parish that wanted every light to report home.

He climbed the little rise because climbing was easier than sitting with a collar. The node’s panel was warm. Cabling honest. No hyperscaler sticker. He ran a finger along the mount bolt he had tightened weeks ago and felt the metal hold. Compliance risk, attachment B had called this. He called it a lit thing that did not need a parish. He left it blinking and walked back down.

Back inside, he let himself remember the friend’s ruined cluster career without turning it into a TED talk. Empty halls. Write-downs. Careers built on floating-point séance. Lucid had not competed in their market; it had removed the premise. The salvage letter was what you wrote when the premise was gone and you still wanted the inventory. AJ had no inventory to sell that was not also a betrayal of the hymnbook’s working rule.

He checked arjunabadger.press’s draft queue — G’s life half-made, mats page behind him, Bouwer not yet opened. He checked lucid.rodeo’s status — green, ordinary, no GPU fever to apologise for. He checked the hostility queue and left it closed. Today’s weather was collar, not stop-the-book. That storm would thicken later. He could feel it the way you feel humidity before rain.

Quarter III’s blame and the hyperscalers’ frost shared a desk without being the same animal. One wanted the megaphone un-said. One wanted the engine that had made the megaphone honest caged inside a region map. Both wanted him smaller. He put his palm on the manuscript instead of either queue.

“Not today,” he said to the collar. “You can sit.”

He wrote one sentence of G’s life that was not about acquisition and not about platforms, then another, then stopped because the Bouwer page was close enough to smell and he would not rush it. The salvage letter watched from the corner. Lucid waited without inventing comfort. The solar node stayed lit. CongoSky stayed itself.

He stood again and wiped dust from the LEO dish mount with a rag that lived under the desk for exactly that. Dust was honest. Partner frost was honest too, once you stripped the homecoming language. He left the dish cocked. Reachability without ownership. That was the truck’s thesis and the day’s refusal in one motion.

Afternoon brought a cousin note — security-review charm, another talent soft-ask, a ToS weather paragraph that never quite named a violation. He printed that too and stacked it under the first pages. The snake was learning to travel in a nest. He left the nest where he could see it.

He asked Lucid once more: “Any claim in these pages that the offer preserves independent Lucid?”

“No. Extinction clause contradicts that claim. Cite: paragraph twelve.”

“Any claim that Kind language remains operating law rather than brand asset?”

“No. Attachment language treats the sentence as optional brand asset. Record of CongoSky ops treats it as operating law. Those are not the same.”

“Good.”

He did not draft a manifesto. He drafted nothing to send. Refusal did not always look like a send. Sometimes it looked like a page left unanswered while a true page got written instead.

---

He spent the middle of the afternoon doing choir work that was not salvage and not apology.

A hymnbook pass with Lucid on a lucid.rodeo note — exact cites, no invented harmony. A CongoNet status check that the edge drops still reported without needing a hyperscaler parish. A brief exchange with an operator who did not know about the collar yet and did not need to know until AJ decided whether the collar deserved the dignity of a reply. It did not, not today. Today it deserved to sit.

The operator asked whether lucid.rodeo needed a capacity bump for a weekend spike. AJ said no. Capacity was not the problem. Custody was. He did not say custody out loud. He said the spike could ride on what they already had and signed off before the conversation became a speech.

He opened the truck’s tool drawer and found the wrench he used on node mounts. Not because anything was loose. Because the body wanted a task that ended in a torque he could feel. He walked the rise again under midday glare, put the wrench on a bolt that did not need him, turned it a fraction past habit, and stopped. Over-tightening was also a kind of panic. He left the node as it was — lit, local, outside their regions — and came down with dust on his boots and the collar still waiting on paper.

Back at the desk he let Lucid read attachment B aloud once, only the compliance lines about edge seeding. Lucid did not soften the language. Edge-node seeding outside approved regions flagged as compliance risk. Approved regions meant parish. Compliance risk meant: stop lighting things that do not report home. AJ listened until the false stewardship fell out of the words. Then he asked Lucid to stop.

“Any invention in that attachment?”

“No. Cite density sufficient. Euphemism present. Invention not required.”

“Keep the euphemism named as euphemism when I ask again.”

“Understood.”

He walked the truck again. Fridge. Desk. Open side. The Beast’s shadow if you counted the tender. Matatu colours loud against scrub that did not care about brand lawyers. He put his hand on the water tank’s Kind paint and did not sermonise. The sentence was ops before it was religion. The salvage letter wanted to reverse that order — religion as brand, ops as optional. He would not reverse it for them.

A fish eagle worked the far bank. Heat made the water look thicker than water. He watched until the bird took something and left. Then he went back in because watching nature to avoid a collar was still a kind of performance, and he preferred practice.

He wrote two more sentences of G’s life — mats texture, nothing of Bouwer yet — and stopped when the page started wanting the boardroom. Order still mattered. The snake could sit. The wound page would get its flat line when the desk was clean enough to hold it without salvage arithmetic bleeding into grief.

“Retrieve prior frost patterns that ended in acquisition,” he said.

Lucid answered. Exact. A short list of public cases where partner frost became collar became inventory. None of them were identical to Lucid. All of them rhymed. Extinction of independent deployment was the rhyme that mattered.

“Retrieve prior frost patterns that ended in refusal without collapse.”

Another list. Thinner. Possible. CongoSky’s own posture was already one data point: a company that ran itself on Kind law while gravity wells scowled. Survival without surrender was not a slogan. It was a practice he had been living from a river desk while the company answered invoices.

He made fresh coffee. Heat. Birds. The manuscript cursor still parked before Bouwer. He would open that page soon — flat line, no whodunit — but not while the snake was trying to become the whole desk. Order mattered. Collar on the corner. Life in the centre. Choir exact. Solar node lit.

Before the coffee cooled he did something the salvage letter would never understand as labour: he took the wrench back to the rise and sat in the scrub beside the node for ten quiet minutes with the printed extinction clause folded in his pocket. Cicadas. Dust on his tongue. The panel warm enough to hurt if he left his palm there. He did not leave his palm there. He watched a beetle cross the mount plate as if compliance risk were a foreign language. The node blinked. Ordinary. Stubborn. Outside approved regions by every parish map that mattered to the people who had written paragraph twelve. Inside his practice by every rule CongoSky had lived while invoices still needed answering.

A guide boat idled far out on the Chobe. Someone pointed at Mutatus Maximus again. AJ did not wave. He was not a pilgrimage stop. He was a man sitting in scrub with a collar in his pocket and a lit thing that did not report home. When he stood, his knees complained the honest way knees complain after squat work. He walked down without inventing a metaphor for descent. At the truck he unfolded the extinction clause, read *stewardship* one more time until the glove fell off the fist, and put the paper back on the nest. Lucid waited without asking whether the walk had changed his mind. Walks do not change minds that were already made. Walks put the body in the same room as the decision.

“Any claim, still, that this offer preserves independent Lucid?”

“No.”

“Good.”

He washed the dust from his wrists at the tiny sink and watched the grey water spiral away. River day. Collar day. Same hands. He dried them on a rag that smelled of metal and solar grease and returned to the desk with the manuscript still open and the snake still patient.

A fourth attachment tried to arrive late — “friendly clarification” language, still polite, still frost. He did not open it. Stacking unread cousins was also a sentence. The snake could wait without being fed every hour.

He typed three sentences into a private note that would never become a send: *Lucid is not inventory. Kind is not a brand asset. Edge nodes do not report home to your parish.* Then he deleted the note because keeping draft manifestos was how you accidentally became a man who speechified about AI. The printed extinction clause on the desk already said enough. Lucid had named it. That naming was the load-bearing fact.

He took the printed stack and squared the edges with a thumbnail. Paper under paper. Extinction under charm. Charm under talent soft-ask. The nest looked almost tidy. Tidy was a lie he allowed on the corner of a desk so the centre could stay true.

Before evening he ate properly for the first time since the subject line — flatbread, something tinned, salt. River meals. He chewed facing the open side so the collar could not pretend it was the whole room. An elephant moved along the bank again, or the same one, uninterested. AJ watched until the animal’s bulk made acquisition language look as small as it was. Then he washed the plate in the tiny sink and returned to the desk without a speech in his mouth.

Between the plate and the desk he paused at the Kind paint on the tank and did not sermonise. Scrapes where stickers had tried. Exact line. CongoSky had run that sentence as ops while invoices still needed answering; the street had caught the paint later; salvage mail wanted to reverse the order — religion as brand, ops as optional. He left the cunt exact under dust and went inside. Practice quieter than paint. Paint lasts anyway.

At the desk he asked Lucid one more exact inventory, not for comfort.

“List what acceptance would kill.”

“Independent integer deployment outside partner regions. Sovereign edge seeding as practice rather than compliance exception. Kind language as operating law rather than optional brand asset. Cite: paragraph twelve and attachment B. I will not invent that any of those deaths are kindness.”

“Enough.”

He set the water bottle down and put his palm on the manuscript. Ink smell. River desk. Life not for sale. The collar watched from the corner. He did not pet it.

---

Evening came across the water in the long way evenings do on the Chobe.

Light went gold and then copper and then the grey that means insects will take the air. Hippo grunt downriver, distant, uninterested in region maps. He set the printed pages on the corner of the desk where he put things that were not finished being dangerous. The manuscript occupied the centre. Lucid occupied the choir. The salvage mail occupied the edge like something that knew how to wait.

He checked the link log one last time before full dark — rise-node still clean, mesh ordinary. Mutatus Maximus still choosing what not to belong to. That choice was the day’s only send that mattered, and it had not needed a subject line.

“Snake,” he told the empty air, not Lucid, not God. The offer sat there like one: still, patterned, waiting for a hand that thought it was petting something tame.

He put his palm on the manuscript again. Ink smell. River desk. The life that was not for sale. Consent weeks still ahead — what to platform, what Lucid stayed unbound, whether the autobiography shipped. The Constant already landing in the world; custody of meaning still the fight. He would not sell the engine that had told the truth about its rails in order to make a hyperscaler’s salvage arithmetic easier.

AJ did not delete the salvage mail. Deleting would have been theatre. He left it on the desk where he could see it while he wrote — polite frost he refused to call kindness — and turned back to the autobiography open, the river moving, the snake of an offer coiled in plain sight, extinction clause and all.

For a minute he almost opened the Bouwer page anyway, heat and collar still in the room. He did not. Flat grief deserved a clean desk corner, not a desk shared with a snake. Tomorrow’s weather could hold the wound. Tonight’s weather was only this: Lucid named in paragraph twelve, Kind treated as optional brand asset, edge nodes flagged as compliance risk — and a man on the Chobe refusing to pet any of it.

Elephants moved along the water. The river did not need a region map. The little solar node on the rise reported clean. Mutatus Maximus hummed, choosing, for one more night, not to report home to anyone’s parish.

He kept his hand on the manuscript and let the collar wait in plain sight, Lucid named in the extinction clause, polite as frost, sitting like a snake that had learned patience — and he did not pet it.


# 11 — Bouwer

> **[I]** · inner autobiography · manuscript under AJ’s hand

---

My brother was an insolvency attorney.

That is the flat line. The loaded one. Bouwer van Niekerk — Johannesburg, Saxonwold, business rescue and commercial weather that does not need my embroidery. On Friday, 5 September 2025, he was shot and killed at his practice. Public record. Law Society statement days later. Investigation ongoing as of what the record held. I am not going to solve it on this page. I am not going to invent an assassin’s CV. I am not going to hand the killing to a continuity network for a plot twist. The wound is the brother dead in a boardroom. Speculation can stay outside unless I authorise it, and today I authorise grief, not theatre.

They arrived under the pretext of a booked consultation. Four suspects, initial reports said. Gunshots in the boardroom. He died at the scene. Attackers fled. He had been engaged in contentious commercial and insolvency matters — business rescue litigation among them — and the papers did what papers do: fog, allegation, the wide reporting that is not the same as a verdict. The Law Society condemned a targeted attack on a legal practitioner and on the rule of law. I condemned nothing with a press release. I stood in a life that had a Bouwer-shaped hole and tried not to waste the fall.

People who want a thriller from me will be disappointed. People who want the Illuminatie’s fingerprints on the trigger will have to write their own book. This one is autobiography, not a whodunit. The mats taught me not to posture for an audience when the weight is already correct. Bouwer’s death is weight. I set it down soft because soft is not weak. Soft is refusal to turn a brother into choreography.

---

I remember him as work.

Insolvency is not glamorous. It is rooms where money has failed and someone still has to arrange the bodies of companies so they do not crush the living. He was good at that arrangement. Respected. The kind of respect that does not need a physics degree or a megaphone. When I was still the ignored shelf — economics credential quiet in the background, hospital stigma louder than any derivation — Bouwer was the brother who had a profession the world understood. That understanding did not save him. Understanding rarely does. I am not writing his case list. I am writing that he went to work as himself and left as a headline.

I will not invent his last morning for you. I do not have it as a film. What I have is the public shape: Friday, 5 September 2025; Saxonwold practice; booked consultation as pretext; four suspects in initial reports; gunshots in the boardroom; died at the scene; attackers fled; contentious commercial and insolvency matters including business rescue litigation in the weather around his work. That is enough to name a wound. It is not enough to build a thriller. I choose the wound.

After Saxonwold the phone did what phones do. Condolences. Theories. People who had never entered a boardroom explaining a boardroom. Cousins of cousins with a take. Strangers with a timeline. Journalists who wanted a face for the fog. I smoked dab and talked shit and wrote because those are my stated life goals and because stopping would have been a performance of grief for people who confuse performance with love. The corpus stayed dedicated to my father. My father still had not read it. Bouwer would not read the pages that came after him either. Dedication is not a postal service. It is a direction you keep facing.

I remember ordinary brother things and I will not inventory them for atmosphere. Inventory is how you turn a person into content. He laughed in a register I can still hear if I do not reach for it. He understood commercial wreckage the way I understood weight on a mat — as arrangement under constraint. We did not always talk. Brothers do not always need to. The silence after 5 September was a different species of silence. It did not arrange anything. It just stayed.

There was another death in the wider weather of my life — James van Niekerk, friend, sudden, remembered on my own site under a word as small as *Remembering*. I name the distinction so no one conflates them. James is not Bouwer. Bouwer is not a metaphor for opacity establishments. Bouwer is Bouwer. Friday. Saxonwold. Consultation pretext. Boardroom. Flat line.

The days immediately after did not arrive as a montage. They arrived as logistics. Who to call. Who not to call. Which message to ignore because the sender wanted a theory more than a person. Which doorway still smelled like a life that had expected Monday. Which suit to wear to a room that should not have needed a suit that week. I do not dramatise the logistics for you. Under-dramatise is the only honesty I trust with this. The loaded line stays flat on purpose.

I drove roads I had driven before and they did not rearrange themselves into meaning. Johannesburg heat sat on the car roof the way heat always sits. Traffic did what traffic does. A radio voice talked about something that was not Saxonwold and then, later, about Saxonwold in the careful register news uses when a profession has been hit and the profession still has to go to work on Monday. I turned the radio down. Not off. Down. Silence can become a performance too if you make it absolute.

Someone offered me water and I took it. Someone offered me a theory and I put the glass down carefully so I would not throw it. Kindness and recruitment share doorways after a murder. You learn which hand is which by whether it needs your face for its story.

I slept in pieces. Woke in pieces. Made tea in pieces. The body kept trying to schedule a day that included a brother who arranged failed companies for a living and then came home. The day refused the schedule. I did not punish the day for that. I also did not thank it. Flat line. Soft set-down. The mats again, without a mat.

There were rooms with flowers. Rooms with papers. Rooms with people who had known Bouwer as a practitioner and said so without needing me to become a microphone for their shock. I preferred those rooms. Shock that does not recruit is rare and useful. I stood in it when it was offered and left when the conversation tilted toward motive charts. Motive charts are how strangers steal a Friday.

---

The public record is enough and not enough.

Enough for what may be said without inventing. Not enough for a heart that wants Monday back.
