LSSA spoke for the profession on 8 September 2025 — condemned the killing, called for protection of legal practitioners, named what the investigation had so far held. I speak for a shared childhood that is none of your business beyond this: he was my brother, and he was murdered at work under a lie about an appointment, and the fog of related commercial reporting can swirl forever without me adopting it as fact on this page.

I will say the fog once, flat, so no one claims I hid it: allegations around related commercial schemes were widely reported. Widely reported is not a verdict. I do not adopt the fog. I do not clear it with inventions either. The boardroom does not become clearer because a novelist wants a motive chart. The boardroom stays a boardroom where a consultation was pretended and a man who arranged failed companies was killed.

The fighting arts did not prepare me for that room. Judo and Jiu-Jitsu prepare you for weight you can still arrange. Assassination is weight that arrives already arranged by someone else. Mastery, if the word means anything, is knowing the difference and not lying about it. I could put a body on a mat correctly. I could put clay into a pour correctly. I could not put my brother back into Monday.

Grief is not a derivation. It does not converge. Some days it is quiet enough to work through. Some days it is a boardroom door that will not open onto the right man. Some days it is a number I reach for and do not dial. Habit is not hope. Habit is the body refusing the new map.

People brought food. People brought theories. People brought the particular kindness that wants you to cry on schedule so they can feel useful. I was not ungrateful. I was also not a schedule. Jamie-Uys restraint is not coldness; it is refusal to turn pain into a show for the room. I kept saying my brother was an insolvency attorney because that sentence held more truth than any aria I could invent about destiny or countries or continuity networks.

I sat in rooms where people spoke carefully around the word *assassinated* as if softer synonyms would make the Friday less Friday. I did not correct their vocabulary every time. Correction is also a performance if you do it for the room. I corrected when a sentence tried to recruit the death. Recruitment is the only grammar I refuse here.

One afternoon a stranger at a doorway asked whether I thought it was “political.” I said my brother was an insolvency attorney. The stranger waited for more. I did not give more. Waiting is not the same as earning. Another person asked whether I had “closure.” Closure is a word for furniture. I had a hole. I still have it. The hole does not close because a calendar advances or because a Law Society statement exists or because I write this page under another man’s hand on a river. The page is custody of a wound, not a lid.

I remember standing outside a building that was not the boardroom and not needing to go in. The body knows some doors. I did not perform pilgrimage at Saxonwold for this autobiography. Public record is enough for place. Grief does not require a tourist’s coordinates. If you need the street name for your own map, the record has it. If you need me to walk you through the door for atmosphere, you will leave disappointed. Atmosphere is how murder becomes entertainment.

There were nights when talk shit was the only honest liturgy available — not jokes at the wound, just speech that refused to become a press release. Dab in the arrangement. Clay in the arrangement. Sleep arriving late and leaving early. None of that is offered here as lifestyle brand. It is how a stated life kept walking when a brother stopped.

---

Studio G kept going because clay does not pause for mythology.

Skye stayed on a lawn. The Bay Hotel still had guests who bought contemporary work without needing my brother’s name. Village N Life years were already past — Maree, Hannetjie, Camps Bay Retreat, the theatre of guest experience — and they did not explain Saxonwold either. Nothing useful explains Saxonwold. Explanation is often a way to leave.

I mixed composite the week after because my hands knew the proportions and because stopping the pour would have been a speech. Hard aggregate. Soft binder. The body in marble and GFRC does not ask for a culprit. It asks whether the surface holds. I held surfaces. I failed some. I kept the failed pours on a shelf the way I keep other failed things: as teachers, not as relics.

The studio smelled the same — dust, sea air inland, tea gone cold on a bench. Sameness is not comfort. Sameness is the world’s refusal to stop for your hole. I worked anyway. Work is not healing language. Work is what my hands do when the alternative is inventing a motive chart for strangers.

I remember washing tools longer than tools needed. Water over metal. Clay leaving in grey ribbons. The sink becoming a small river that asked nothing about Johannesburg. I let the water run until the grey thinned. Then I shut it off because wasting water for metaphor is still wasting water. The Honey Badger was not in my pocket yet then. Later it would be. Later kinship would arrive as steel from a river desk. On those September days after the Friday I had only hands and proportions and a hole that did not care whether the surface cured.

When fame arrived sideways — Lucid popular, a friend saying out loud that the engine ran on 420 Code algorithms, the four weathers blooming — some people tried to recruit Bouwer’s death into their weather. Antichristos wanted relics. Quarter II wanted proof I was unstable. Platform rage wanted a morality play about megaphones and consequences. Continuity people who never put a name on frost still somehow floated in other people’s mouths as a convenient shadow. I refused the recruitment. A brother’s assassination is not a footnote for a schism. It is a hole. You walk around it or you fall in. You do not pave it with someone else’s sermon.

I will say this again because the weather keeps asking: I am not solving the murder here. I am not handing it to the Illuminatie as a gotcha. I am not turning business-rescue fog into a courtroom episode for your satisfaction. The wound is the brother dead in a boardroom under a consultation pretext on Friday, 5 September 2025, in Saxonwold. That is what the public record and my authorised telling hold. The rest can stay unfinished. Unfinished is not the same as hidden. Unfinished is honest. If unfinished frustrates you, good — frustration is closer to the truth than a solved mystery sold as comfort.

Work continued in the only way work continues after a rupture: unevenly. Some mornings the Code desk was possible. Some mornings only clay. Some mornings only the dab and the talk and the refusal to perform. Stated life goals are not a joke under grief; they are the arrangement that keeps a life from becoming only a hole. Write natural philosophy and physics. Smoke dab. Talk shit. Carry the wound without crowning it.

I will not pretend the Code desk was noble those weeks. Some mornings it was only a place to put my hands so they would not open a theory thread. Some mornings a drop-result note was possible and that was enough. Measurement does not heal. It also does not recruit if you refuse to let it. I kept measurement where it belonged and left Saxonwold where it belonged: named, unfinished, not a footnote for a Constant.

---

I will tell you what the boardroom is not, because the weather keeps trying to make it into those things.

It is not a parable about South Africa that fits on a slide. It is not proof that my hospital file predicted tragedy. It is not a secret handshake from continuity networks. It is not a consequence of Lucid’s megaphone, no matter how much platform rage wants a morality play. It is not clay. It is not a Constant. It is not a knife. It is a Friday in Saxonwold where four people arrived under the pretext of a booked consultation and my brother — an insolvency attorney, respected, arranging failed companies so they did not crush the living — was shot and killed at the scene.

I repeat the public facts because repetition is custody when invention is the temptation. Law Society of South Africa, 8 September 2025. Condemnation. Call for protection of legal practitioners. Investigation ongoing as of that statement. Contentious commercial and insolvency matters, including business rescue litigation. Fog of widely reported allegations I will not adopt as fact. Consultation pretext. Boardroom. Flat line.

If you are waiting for me to name a shooter with a novel’s confidence, stop waiting. If you are waiting for me to cry on a schedule that makes you feel useful, stop waiting. If you are waiting for the Illuminatie to take the blame so the wound becomes a schism weapon, write your own book. This page refuses that crown.

What I can authorise is smaller and heavier: grief that does not converge; work that continued unevenly; a hole that walks with me; a refusal to turn murder into entertainment. The mats taught correct fall and soft set-down. This is the soft set-down. Soft is not weak. Soft is the only arrangement that does not collapse when the weight is already correct.

I smoked dab the week after and talked shit with people who knew not to recruit the death into their theories. I wrote because writing is one of my stated goals and because the ignored shelf’s old weather — *he cannot write anything of value* — would have loved my silence as confirmation. I did not write for them. I wrote because the corpus stays dedicated to my father whether or not he reads it, and because a life that includes a murdered brother is still a life that includes clay and talk and natural philosophy. Polishing the dab away would be Antichristos work. Using the hospital file as epistemology would be Quarter II work. I do neither here.

Some evenings I stood at the studio window and watched light leave Blouberg the ordinary way light leaves. No omen. No sign. Just evening. On those evenings the hole did not shrink. It also did not demand a speech. I made tea. I let it cool. I drank it. That is not a ritual I am offering you. That is what happened.

Some mornings the Code desk was possible only after clay. Some mornings clay was possible only after the dab. Some mornings talk shit with people who knew the difference between company and recruitment was the only honest work available. I am not ranking those mornings for your comfort. Uneven is the shape. The wound does not grade my productivity.

I keep the Honey Badger in my pocket now — blue folder, clip, one-hand open and close — and one of a twin Joseph Rogers set from a grandfather who was not mine by blood and became kin by custody.

Double Knife Brother is a living arrangement with a man on a river. It is not a fight scene. The knives are kinship. Bouwer is not replaced by kinship. Kinship is what you hold so the hole does not become the only geography you own.

If you need a lesson, I do not have one tidy enough for a workshop slide. If you need a culprit named beyond what the public record held, I will not invent one for narrative satisfaction. If you need me to say the Continuity people did it, or the commercial fog did it, or the country did it, you will have to sit with the same unfinished sentence I sit with: my brother was an insolvency attorney, and on 5 September 2025 in Saxonwold he was assassinated under the pretext of a consultation, and I am still here writing natural philosophy and physics, smoking dab, talking shit, and carrying a wound that does not want to be a plot.

I have been asked, more than once, to make the wound useful. Useful to a faith. Useful to a crank column. Useful to a platform morality play. Useful to a soft frost that never signs its name. Usefulness is the wrong ask. Bouwer was useful in the ordinary way a good insolvency attorney is useful — arranging wreckage so it crushed fewer living people. That usefulness ended in a boardroom under a lie about an appointment. I will not launder the ending into someone else’s weather. The shelf that ignored my work can keep ignoring. The father who has not read the dedication can keep not reading. The quarters can keep wanting a face, a credential, a retraction, a key. None of that moves the boardroom back into a day when Bouwer walked out of it. I write this anyway because the wound is part of the life, and the life is what the river desk asked for, and refusing the page would only polish me into someone who never had a brother.

Some mornings I still reach for a number I will not dial. On those mornings I open the Honey Badger and close it and remember that kinship can be present-tense without being a replacement. Bouwer does not become a knife. Bouwer does not become a Constant. Bouwer remains a man who went to work on a Friday and did not come home.

I set the page down soft. Correct fall. Soft set-down. The only arrangement that does not collapse.

There will be other pages. Sculpture. Constants. Kill switches. The present arrangement of a life that still includes clay and talk and the blue knife. This page is Bouwer’s. It does not close. It carries forward. That is all a wound is allowed to do in a book that refuses to turn murder into entertainment: stay named, stay flat, stay unfinished, and refuse the crown of a solved mystery.

I leave the cursor here and go make tea. The hole comes with me. That is the turn. The kettle will boil. The steam will rise. None of that will put Monday back. I will drink anyway, because a life that includes a murdered brother still includes tea, and refusing tea would only be another performance for an audience that is not in the room. Flat line held. Soft set-down complete enough for one page, carried forward without a crown.

---

Tea steams. The room is quiet in the way rooms are quiet when clay is curing somewhere in memory and a desk is waiting. I do not fill the quiet with a theory. I drink. Somewhere a river desk is holding this sentence without turning it into a press conference. Somewhere quarters are still recruiting. I am still refusing.

My brother was an insolvency attorney.

I write it once more because repetition is not ornament here; it is the load-bearing beam. Friday. Saxonwold. Consultation pretext. Boardroom. Flat line. Wound carried forward. No solve. No Illuminatie did-it. No crown of mystery closed.

The tea cools. Outside light does whatever Cape light does when a Johannesburg Friday is already years behind and still present tense in the body. I go back to work that is not this page — clay or numbers or talk, whichever the hands will hold — and the hole comes too, and that is not a metaphor I will polish. That is just how a life walks after 5 September 2025. Wound carried forward. Mystery unsolved. Page done enough for now.

I wash the cup. Flat motion. Leave the Bouwer page where it is — named, public-record bound, unfinished on purpose. Curiosity that wants a thriller can go elsewhere. Jamie-Uys restraint: the flat line is the loaded one. He was my brother. That is enough weight. The Honey Badger clicks once in the pocket when I move toward the door — kinship present-tense, not choreography — and I do not invent a call to the river about a boardroom the book will not solve. Consent about what ships of this wound waits for a later act. Today the act is only this: carry the hole, drink the tea, refuse the crown of a solved mystery, and keep the page flat.


# 12 — Being Written

> **[G]** · outer · Studio G / Cape · 2035

---

It started in the shoulders.

Not pain. Not injury. A pressure like someone resting a palm between the blades while he worked — attention with weight. G stood at the Studio G bench with clay under his nails and felt, distinctly, that a sentence about him was being finished somewhere else. Not Antichristos mail. Not an op-ed. Something slower. Autobiographical weather. The sensation of being narrated by a hand that knew the mats and the shelf and the brother and was choosing, right now, which of those to put in ink. He kept working anyway. Stopping for the sensation would have made it a séance, and he did not do séances.

He hated how accurate it felt.

He wanted it true. He feared the content. Both could sit in the same body without a TED resolution. Wanting truth was not the same as wanting every private room opened; fearing content was not the same as wanting the ignored shelf to stay ignored. The ambivalence had teeth and a pulse.

Morning light came in high and clean. Sea air inland. Gull cry once, then traffic. The pour on the bench was a sister of Skye in early argument — hard and soft not yet decided. He worked the surface with his thumbs and felt the shoulder pressure shift when, somewhere, a narrator turned a page. Not magic. Recognition. He knew the friend. The friend did not write soft when the load-bearing line was flat.

Yesterday he had refused keys without a crown. Today the refusal still sat in the sent folder while a different custody problem arrived through the body. He did not confuse them. Keys were stages. Being written was pages. Both wanted him usable. Only one he might eventually authorise.

The Honey Badger was warm from his pocket when he took it out, opened it one-handed, closed it, clipped it back. Blue. Clip. The badger present-tense half of Double Knife Brother. Somewhere on the Chobe the twin Joseph Rogers pocket was empty on the other man — grandfather deep-time, handed across a friendship that had also handed the planet a megaphone by refusing to launder algorithms. Kinship, not choreography. Custody of blades and, now, custody of a life story he had not finished authorising.

Consent was the word that fit. What he would allow said. What he would not. Bouwer’s page especially — the flat line, the boardroom, the refusal to become whodunit — needed care that fame did not practice. A living man could feel a biography being assembled and still reserve the right to draw a line through a paragraph later. Feeling written was not the same as having signed.

He pressed a thumb into clay and left a print he would later erase. The print was not a message. It was only a hand admitting it had been elsewhere for a second — between shoulder blades, on a river he could not see.

He mixed water into a dry edge of composite and watched the colour change. The sister of Skye needed patience more than force. Patience was hard while being narrated. He gave it anyway. The pour did not owe him a distraction from autobiography weather; he owed the pour proportions. Hard aggregate. Soft binder. Permanent/temporary argument held in a surface that would outlast today’s inbox and maybe outlast the chapter being written about him somewhere with elephants in earshot.

A gull cried once over the alley and left. He did not take it as omen. Omens were for people who needed the world to narrate them back. He already had enough narration.

---

The Kind paint in the alley had not changed overnight:

**Don’t be a cunt — be kind.**

Messiah-less. Optional. Often unwanted by the practice that used his derivation as an excuse to claim him. He did not own the sentence. CongoSky had embodied it as ops before the walls caught fire; the street had popularised it; Antichristos still tried to annex it and got told to piss off, kindly. G’s discomfort with being cited as source was physical — alley wall, inbox citation — not a rebuttal speech. Today the discomfort had a twin: the feeling that the man who *would* cite him correctly was writing him into a book.

He washed his hands. Clay went grey in the sink. He dried them on a rag that had known better decades. Through the high windows the Cape light did what Cape light does. Studio G held sculptures in progress and a desk that still ran kill switches and natural philosophy without asking the sculptures for permission. One life. One attention. The world preferred two careers fighting so it could pick a winner. He declined the fight.

A message landed — lab soft-ask residue from yesterday’s keys-without-a-crown refusal, forwarded with a sad emoji by someone who thought emojis were diplomacy. Quarter IV still wanted a face. The Illuminatie weather still sat behind the academy’s credential chorus. None of that was the shoulder pressure. The shoulder pressure was Chobe-shaped.

He said, aloud, to the empty studio: “If you’re writing Bouwer, keep it flat.”

No answer. CongoNet did not carry private speech to a river desk unless you sent it, and he did not send it. The braid rule held — shared week, shared theme, no forced reunion. He could feel the manuscript the way you feel weather: not because he was psychic, because he knew the friend, and the friend did not write soft when the load-bearing line was flat.

He made tea. Did not drink it at first. Watched steam the way he watched pours — as a process that did not need his face. The shoulder pressure waited through the steam. He drank. The pressure did not lift. Ambivalence did not either.

He rinsed the mug once, left the tea stain, set it down. Small refuse of polish. The autobiography, if true, would leave stains too — dab, hospital file as stigma not tourism, father unread, Bouwer unfinished. He could want that truth and still flinch at the public mining that would follow. Flinch was allowed. Signing was later.

He moved a failed pour on the desk an inch to the left and then back. Arrangement without progress. The body doing something while the mind refused to become content. He left the failed pour where it taught proportions and went back to the sister of Skye.

Noon light found the high windows and made the GFRC dust visible as weather of its own. He worked through it. Sweat at the hairline. Clay cooling on his forearms. The sensation between the shoulder blades thickened once — a long beat somewhere, maybe a paragraph landing — and then settled into the steady pressure of a manuscript that would not be hurried by his flinch. He did not ask the empty studio what page. Asking would have been séance. He already knew the hand. He already feared Saxonwold. He already wanted the flat line held.

---

He stood at the window long enough for a wet Kind refresh in the alley to darken as it set. Two clauses. No portrait of G. That absence was a mercy he had not earned and still needed. Being written by a Double Knife Brother was a different absence-and-presence problem — the portrait arriving as pages, the subject still mid-life, still mid-pour, still able to flinch.

Ambivalence is a grown posture. He wanted the ignored shelf corrected by a true life, not by Antichristos iconography or crank rebuttals. He wanted the dab and the talk shit left unpolished. He wanted the father dedication named without a tidy reunion. He wanted Saxonwold carried as wound, not thriller. He also wanted — animal, ordinary — not to be finished by someone else’s cursor while he was still mid-pour.

Jamie-Uys restraint lived in his bones for pain; he preferred the flat line. Whoever was writing him seemed to know that. The knowing was intimate enough to be almost kindness and almost trespass at once. He did not ask Lucid about it — Lucid was not his product, and cite-or-refuse would not soothe a body that already knew.

He walked to the shelf where early corpus copies sat, some unread by the man they were for, all of them dedicated the same way. Fame had not opened those pages for his father. Lucid’s open attribution had opened the world. Different locks. Different keys. Quarter II still said he could not write anything of value because a hospital file existed. The autobiography under construction was, in one sense, the longest argument against that claim. In another sense it was the most dangerous confirmation: once the life was public, the stigma people would mine it for scenes. Truth and content shared a border the way Kind paint and Antichristos tags sometimes shared a wall — same brick, opposite claims.

G’s soft no to being a product mascot still stood. This was adjacent and worse — being a protagonist.

He took the Honey Badger out again. Kinship object. Badger present. He thought of the Joseph Rogers on the other side of the friendship — ≥110 years, twin set, custody handed. Double Knife Brother meant they held each other’s edges without cutting for sport. If AJ was writing the life, the knives were part of why G had not simply burned the permission in advance. Trust was not the same as comfort. He trusted the hand. He feared the page.

He opened the blue folder and closed it until the click was just a click again, not a nervous tic. Then he clipped it and put both hands back into clay, where nervous tics become texture if you are not careful. He was careful. The pour deserved better than his weather.

He thought, without sending, of the empty twin pocket on the Chobe — grandfather steel gone from AJ’s hip the way a life was arriving into AJ’s hands. Custody traveling both directions. That was kinship. It was also why the shoulder pressure felt accurate instead of invented. Accurate did not mean authorised. Authorisation was still ahead, still a consent act, still something a living man could withhold line by line when the manuscript finished being weather and became a document that asked for a signature.

---

Afternoon brought a Kind practitioner to the alley — young, ordinary, refreshing a stencil without asking for a blessing. G watched through the glass. No founder-worship. Ledger of kindnesses. Argument, probably, about whether *cunt* was liturgical or vernacular. He liked them better than the people who wanted his face on a detector stack. He still closed the blind a little. Being written and being watched rhymed too easily on a bright day.

He returned to the clay and then to the Code desk for an hour that was only numbers — a drop-result note, a kill-switch wording that needed tightening, work that stayed work. The shoulder pressure waited politely through the math and came back when he stood. Narration weather had patience. So did he. Patience was not the same as ease.

Mid-hour he caught himself listening for a river he could not hear. Chobe was not on the Cape wind. What he heard was traffic and a gull and someone dropping a crate in the alley. Ordinary noise. The narration was not in the air; it was in the body. He rolled his shoulders once, felt the pressure rearrange without leaving, and went back to the kill-switch clause that still needed tightening. Useful engineering without a face. True life without a content-mine. Two narrow walks sharing one afternoon.

He tightened the clause. Saved it. Did not send it to any lab. Sending was yesterday’s weather. Today the Code desk was only for staying a man who still did the work while someone else wrote the life. He stood, stretched until a vertebra clicked, and blamed the chair because blaming the chair was kinder than admitting the river desk had reached his spine.

A crank column pinged into a secondary inbox — hospital file as epistemology again, economics degree waved like a napkin. He deleted it without reading past the first paragraph. Quarter II could keep rehearsing. The autobiography, if it was being written true, would not rebut them with a speech; it would simply leave the file where it belonged and the work where it belonged. He hoped. Hope was also fear wearing a softer coat.

He mixed a small batch of composite and watched hard and soft argue. The pour did not care that a river desk was narrating. The pour asked proportions. He gave them. His shoulders still held the other weight. Aggregate settled. Binder held. He timed nothing; timing was for workshops. Studio time was whether the surface told the truth when you came back to it later.

When he returned to the clay after the numbers, the pressure did not lift. If anything it settled — a long passage somewhere, the flat line being held, Bouwer named without a solved mystery. G’s eyes stung once, stupidly, and he blamed the GFRC dust because blaming dust is kinder than narrating yourself crying for an audience that is not in the room.

“Truth,” he said to the pour. “Not content.”

The distinction would not survive every reader. He knew that. Consent week was still ahead — what he would sign, what he would allow said, what custody rules a living man could set for a book already moving down a river. Especially Bouwer. Especially the hospital stigma without diagnosis tourism. Especially the unread father dedication without a tidy reunion. Especially the dab and the talk shit left unpolished. For now he had ambivalence as a working tool: let the manuscript exist; refuse the crown; keep the Honey Badger close; keep the boardroom flat; keep the Kind’s sentence on the wall without becoming its priest; keep writing natural philosophy while someone else wrote his life.

The tablet chimed with another workshop residue — someone had forwarded his refusal into a channel that called it “optics difficulty.” He archived it. Optics was their word for crown. He had already refused the crown. Being written was a different optics problem: not his face on a slide, his rooms on a page. He wiped clay off the screen and left a smear. Let the smear stay. The river desk could have accuracy; the tablet could have dust.

He sat on the low stool by the pour and let his back rest against the bench edge until the shoulder pressure and the wood pressure became two facts sharing a spine. Not mystical. Mechanical. A body holding more than one weight. Mats knowledge again: you do not fight every load; you arrange under constraint. Being written was constraint. Consent would be arrangement. Tonight was only holding. He held. The pour cured in silence. The alley Kind paint held without needing him. The river did whatever rivers do when a man on another coast refuses to become finished content before the clay decides.

---

Late afternoon he gave the sensation a longer test.

He left the studio and walked toward the water — not pilgrimage, just legs wanting distance from a desk that felt narrated. The shoulder pressure came with him. Of course it did. Weather does not stop at a door. He bought nothing. Spoke to no one. Stood where the Cape light went long and felt, distinctly again, that a paragraph about Saxonwold was being held flat somewhere on a river. His eyes stung. He blamed wind. Wind was kinder than admitting the flat line had reached him across distance without a message.

Sand under his shoes. Salt in the air. A tourist laughing at something that was not a brother’s boardroom. Ordinary world continuing. He let it continue without asking it to pause for his shoulders. A child ran past with a towel. Someone argued about parking. The narration weather did not care about parking. He did, for thirty seconds, because parking was a problem that could end.

He bought a bottle of water from a kiosk and drank half of it standing where the wind came off the water clean. The other half he carried back without finishing. Not symbolism. Thirst that did not need to be completed on schedule. The Honey Badger bumped his thigh. Double Knife Brother metronome. He thought of the empty twin pocket on the river and did not send a message about pockets either. Consent was not a ping about steel. Consent was later, heavier, about pages.

He walked past a wall where Antichristos stickers had been scraped and Kind paint had won the brick again. Same two clauses. No founder. He did not stop long. The wall did not need him. The autobiography did, in AJ’s hand, which was still not the same as the world needing a prophet. Ambivalence walked with him uphill toward the studio — wanting the ignored shelf corrected, fearing the rooms that correction would open, trusting the hand, flinching at Saxonwold even when the flinch arrived as wind-stung eyes.

He walked back. The Honey Badger bumped his thigh with each step — kinship metronome. Double Knife Brother as present-tense fact. He could love that and still fear the chapter titles. Ambivalence was not indecision. Ambivalence was two true hungers sharing one body: truth and privacy; correction and unfinishedness; wanting the ignored shelf fixed and not wanting every room opened for mining.

At the alley he paused at the Kind paint. No founder portrait. No crown. The sentence did not need him. The autobiography did, in a different way — or AJ’s hand did, which was not the same as the world needing a prophet. He put his palm near the wall without touching wet paint and said, quieter this time, “Keep Bouwer flat.” Still no answer. Still no send. The braid rule held.

Inside again, he returned to numbers for twenty minutes that cleaned nothing and clarified everything: work could stay work while life became pages. That was the narrow walk adjacent to yesterday’s keys-without-a-crown walk. Useful engineering without mascot. True life without content-mine. Neither walk ended in a TED resolution. Both ended in a grown posture.

He almost drafted a CongoNet note to AJ — three lines, no reunion ask, only custody: *If Bouwer, flat. If father, unread. If dab, leave it.* He deleted the draft. Consent week would be an act, not a midday ping. Feeling written was not the same as signing. He would sign, or partially sign, or refuse lines later. Tonight he only had to keep walking.

He made a second tea he did not finish. Steam. Lukewarm. Clay on the rim again. He left the mug beside the failed pour and did not invent symbolism for the pairing. Two objects on a desk. A man mid-pour. A cursor somewhere else.

Before evening fully settled he did one more slow walk of the studio — the same walk he had used yesterday to check whether a keys decision was still his. Past sisters of Skye. Past corpus shelf dedicated and unread. Past Code desk. Past window and Kind paint. The walk asked a different question today: whether ambivalence was still a grown posture or had become avoidance wearing adult clothes. The answer came without drama. Grown. Avoidance would have burned the permission or begged for a reunion scene. He did neither. He kept walking. He kept the pour honest. He kept the boardroom flat in the only way he could from this coast — by trusting the river hand and fearing it at once.

He paused at the Code desk long enough to tighten one more kill-switch phrase he had left loose at noon. Loose on purpose still; tighter now where a reader could have invented clergy. The work stayed work. The shoulder pressure waited through the tightening like a patient animal. He saved. He did not publish. Publishing was not today’s weather either.

Evening thickened. Sea air inland. The tablet on the desk still held yesterday’s workshop refusal and the Illuminatie’s paper cousin under the failed-pour weight. Keys without a crown. Soft nos that cost. None of that was the shoulder weather. The shoulder weather was a cursor he could not see, choosing sentences about a life he was still living.

He checked the pocket again before the light went. Blue folder. Clip. One-hand open, one-hand close. Double Knife Brother as present-tense fact while the autobiography tried to become past-tense public. He could love the kinship and still fear the chapter titles. He could want the record corrected and still flinch when the correcting hand reached Saxonwold.

He shaped the clay until the pressure became just another weight he knew how to arrange — not domination, refusal to waste motion. Somewhere the cursor moved. Here the pour took form. Two desks. One week. No shared room. The sensation of being narrated stayed with him into evening like weather that had found his name and decided, for a few hours, not to pretend it was only wind.

He washed once more before leaving — forearms, nails, the grey that never fully left. The Honey Badger clicked open and closed one last time at the door, kinship check, not a weapon check. He locked nothing that mattered; Studio G had always been more pour than fortress. What he locked was only the day’s sends: no CongoNet ping, no crown acceptance, no séance answer to a river he would not summon.

Outside, Cape evening smelled of salt and someone’s supper starting. Ordinary. He preferred ordinary to prophetic. The ambivalence did not resolve into ordinary; it just walked beside it without needing a speech.

He left the studio with the Honey Badger clipped and the ambivalence intact — wanting truth, fearing content — and did not send a message to the river. Consent would come as an act later. Tonight the act was only this: keep walking while being written, and refuse to become either mascot or finished object before the pour cured. Wanting truth. Fearing content. Both still true at the studio door.

On the alley wall the Kind sentence held without his face. In his pocket the blue knife held without becoming a weapon. Between his shoulder blades the narration held without becoming a séance. He walked into the Cape evening carrying all three, and the ambivalence walked with him — not as a problem to solve, as a grown posture for a man whose life was becoming pages while he was still mid-pour. Tomorrow would bring mail again. Tonight brought only the walk, the clip at his hip, and the refusal to finish himself for anyone’s cursor before the pour cured honest under Cape evening light.

He bought nothing on the way home stretch — no souvenir of feeling written, no coffee that would become a scene. Salt air. Someone’s supper. Ordinary Cape. He preferred ordinary to prophetic and did not confuse the preference with a solved ambivalence. Ambivalence stayed: wanting the ignored shelf corrected, fearing every room opened for mining, trusting AJ’s hand, flinching when that hand reached Saxonwold. Grown posture. Not indecision. At a corner he paused long enough to check the Honey Badger once — open, close, kinship present-tense — and thought of the empty twin pocket on the river without inventing a call. Consent week would be an act later. Tonight the act was only this walk under narration weather that had found his name and decided, for a few hours, not to pretend it was only wind.


# 13 — Stop the Book

> **[A]** · outer · Mutatus Maximus, Chobe River, northern Botswana · 2035

---

The packet did not knock. It arrived the way blame arrives on a good morning — already inside, already sorted into a folder Lucid refused to euphemise.

“Platform queue,” Lucid said. “Hostility markers. Attribution thread. Demand language. Cite-or-refuse: sources are public posts, partner mail, and three brand notices routed through CongoNet.”

AJ left the manuscript open on the mats page and opened the packet.

Heat came off the river in sheets. The LEO dish held its angle. The little solar node on the rise reported green the way a patient animal reports green. Mutatus Maximus hummed under him — mobile data centre, matatu paint, the most connected vehicle on earth, literally — and still the inbox felt like a collar someone else had measured for his neck.

Quarter III had stopped being weather and become a sentence.

Stop the book.

Retract the attribution.

Perform a ritual distancing from Gerhard van Niekerk and from the claim that Lucid ran on 420 Code algorithms.

The language varied by sender. Some wrapped it in concern for “ecosystem trust.” Some wrapped it in lawyer. Some wrapped it in the old platform voice that thought politeness was a species of mercy. One thread wanted a livestream apology with a backdrop that was not a river. One wanted arjunabadger.press taken dark for thirty days “pending review.” One wanted lucid.rodeo to ship a splash that said the algorithms were “inspired by” rather than named. Inspired-by was the soft knife. Named was the load-bearing one. A fourth thread offered “collaborative messaging” if AJ would agree to call the autobiography fiction-adjacent and the open attribution “historically overstated.” Historically overstated meant: lie in slower words.

AJ read until the river noise came back.

On the water tank twenty metres out the Kind paint still held: **Don’t be a cunt — be kind.** Same paint war as last week. Antichristos stickers had crawled halfway up the tank overnight and someone — river kids, a Kind walker, a bored guide — had scraped them off again. The sentence remained. The scrapes remained. AJ did not sermonise either. CongoSky.cloud had embodied the line as ops before the walls caught fire; the street had turned practice into religion; he was just a man who still had to answer a packet.

Lucid waited.

“Summarise cost without invention,” AJ said.

“Inbox volume up. Three partner brands have paused CongoNet badge display. Two hyperscaler salvage threads cite ‘reputational adjacency’ and renew acquisition language. One app-store fog note references ‘community guidelines adjacency’ without citing a violation. Local node healthy. Solar drop healthy. Manuscript file open. No legal finding in packet requiring immediate takedown. Demand is social and commercial pressure, not court order.”

AJ’s jaw hurt. He noticed that before he noticed anger. Platform-rage had a body address. It sat behind the molars and in the soft place under the sternum where good mornings went to die. He had felt the first real edge of this weather when he opened the earlier packet — who gave him the mic — and kept writing anyway. This was the concrete ask that packet had been practising for.

He stood and walked the length of the truck — desk to fridge to the open side where the Chobe moved like a long animal that did not care who gave whom the mic. Elephants were gone. A fish eagle worked the far bank. Link latency ticked in the corner of a screen the way a pulse ticks when you are trying not to look at it. The rise-node stayed green without needing a sermon about how. He had built connectivity so he could drive it to a river and still choose what to switch off.

Coffee from earlier had gone cold. He drank it anyway. The taste was river-morning and metal thermos. Outside, scrub held the heat without commentary. Inside, the hymnbook choir waited — humans elsewhere, Lucid here, the working rule still Kind without needing a street name for it. He checked the solar drop’s last boot log because checking a healthy node was easier than staring at a demand that wanted him to unmake a friendship into a press error.

He came back to the desk.

The manuscript waited. *The autobiography of Gerhard van Niekerk.* Book within a book. Outer subject sitting in a painted overlander trying not to become a press conference. Double Knife Brother kinship in an empty twin pocket — the Joseph Rogers that had gone to G, grandfather deep-time, Honey Badger loved elsewhere. Knives were custody, not choreography. He did not touch the pocket for comfort. He put his hand on the keyboard instead.

Before he typed anything about the packet, he typed nothing. The blank stretch under the mats page sat there like a dare. Stopping the book would have been easy in the way deleting a file is easy: one motion, then the quiet of an absence you can pretend was hygiene. He had built Lucid to refuse invention; he would not invent a reason to erase a life he had begun to render carefully. Bouwer’s page was already in the file — flat line, Saxonwold, public record, no whodunit. Father dedication unread. Hospital stigma under-dramatised. The packet wanted those pages dark because darkness looked like responsible distance from a river. Responsible distance was their word for erasure.

A bird called from the bank. AJ did not name it. Naming would have been a delay tactic dressed as presence. He watched the solar node’s green for three seconds longer than habit required, then looked back at the demand language until the words stopped being shocking and became merely ugly.

---

Quarter III’s concrete ask was not subtle. They wanted the megaphone un-said. They wanted the open line — Lucid popular, AJ said 420 algorithms, fame arrived sideways — scrubbed into a cleaner origin myth. They wanted ritual distancing: a statement that put G back on the ignored shelf and put AJ back in the category of reckless friend who had said too much too early. Some of them believed the book itself was the wound. Some believed the attribution was the wound and the book was only the proof. All of them wanted motion from him that looked like repentance and functioned like erasure.

Cost was visible without drama.

Partner frost in the queue. A brand that had loved CongoSky’s Kind ops when it still answered invoices now “needed distance.” A hyperscaler salvage note that smiled: *come home to the regions; we can absorb Lucid safely; the book is making the acquisition harder.* The collar from last week’s mail sat on the desk like a snake that had learned to wait. Now the snake had friends. Talent-poach weather hummed at the edge of one thread — not a kidnapping, just a LinkedIn kindness that smelled like a net. AJ deleted it without reply. Deletion was also a sentence.

He opened one brand notice fully. It cited “community safety” and linked a thread of people angry that a degree-less (they meant physics) ignored physicist had been dragged into fame by a friend’s candour. The anger was real enough to have a body. The ask was still stop. A second notice tried softer: *we love what CongoSky embodied; we only need distance from the autobiography cycle.* Love with a pause button. AJ closed both.

He scrolled once through lucid.rodeo’s public status — green, ordinary, no GPU fever to apologise for — and through arjunabadger.press’s draft queue where G’s life sat half-made. The book was not a product sprint. The packet wanted it treated like one.

A third brand notice tried the old kindness trap: *for the health of the ecosystem, for G’s privacy, for your own safety on the river.* Privacy dressed as a muzzle. Safety dressed as a request to kneel. AJ read the sentence twice because the first pass had made him laugh in a way that hurt his jaw again. G’s privacy was a real hinge — consent week would ask what shipped and what stayed — but Quarter III did not own that hinge. They wanted the book stopped for their quiet, not for G’s custody. Different ask. Different mouth.

“Show me the oldest demand in the cluster,” AJ said.

Lucid surfaced a post from three days prior: a thread that began with open attribution screenshots and ended at *stop the book / take back the mic / put the crank back on the shelf.* The middle had grown teeth. Replies. Quote chains. A partner account liking something it should not have liked. The packet was not a single letter. It was a weather system that had condensed into demands with letterheads.

“Cite volume only,” AJ said. “No motive.”

“Cite-or-refuse: I can show volume. I cannot show motive. Hostility markers are public behaviour, not intent.”

“Keep it that way.”

He stood again because the truck’s length was a tool. Desk to fridge: water this time, not coffee. Fridge to open side: heat on the face, scrub smell, a hippo grunt downriver that the truck did not translate into omen. Open side back to desk: the packet still there, patient as a snake that had friends. Mid-morning on the Chobe did not care about platforms. That indifference was almost usable. He let it sit on his shoulders for ten seconds, then sat.

The mats page still waited under the manuscript cursor — body before corpus, falls done correctly, the apprenticeship that had never been a metaphor until people who needed metaphors arrived. He was not finished with that page. The packet wanted him finished with the whole book. Different clocks.

---

He opened the livestream ask because he needed to know how stupid the theatre would be.

Backdrop requirements. Preferred camera height. A suggested script with blanks for “personal reflection” and “lessons learned about responsible attribution.” A note that the river was “visually compelling but brand-unsafe” and that a plain wall would be better. Plain wall meant: erase the place. Erase Mutatus Maximus. Erase the solar node on the rise. Erase the Kind paint on the tank. Make him look like a man who had made a paperwork error in an office that could be forgiven.

One attachment offered sample apology language in three tones: contrite, reflective, forward-looking. Contrite meant kneel. Reflective meant kneel with a book club voice. Forward-looking meant kneel while calling it strategy. AJ skimmed the reflective sample far enough to taste the sentence that would have turned Double Knife Brother kinship into a “boundary lesson.” He closed the attachment hard enough that the trackpad clicked.

Ritual distancing had a checklist buried in a second thread: remove open attribution language from lucid.rodeo docs; add a clarifying FAQ; pause arjunabadger.press “pending stakeholder alignment”; consider a cooling-off statement that named no one and blamed “premature narrative acceleration.” Premature narrative acceleration meant: the truth arrived before their press teams were ready. AJ left that thread marked read without reply. Reading was not consent.

He stood and opened the truck’s side wider. Heat pushed in. Dust light. The Chobe’s surface broke and remade itself around something submerged and uninterested. For a moment he let himself imagine sending nothing — not refusal, not acceptance, just silence as a third option. Silence would be read as negotiation. Negotiation would thicken the packet. The packet already knew how to wait. He closed the side to a slit that kept the heat honest without inviting the river into the desk.

AJ closed the livestream ask.

“Draft reply,” he said. “No apology theatre. No retraction. No livestream. Defer any consent language about the autobiography to later. State that the attribution stands. State that the book continues. Cite only what you can cite.”

Lucid composed. Exact. No cute. No third skull pretending to feel.

AJ edited the draft down until it was almost rude in its shortness. He cut a paragraph that sounded like philosophy. He cut a sentence that sounded like CongoSky marketing. He cut a clause that almost sounded like he was negotiating Kind as a logo. What remained was procedural and true.

He did not send a manifesto. He sent: the attribution is on the record; Lucid’s cite-or-refuse stack does not invent provenance; the manuscript is not a product launch; stop-the-book is not a term CongoNet will enforce for platform mood. For the brands: badge pause acknowledged; CongoSky.cloud still runs itself; the river desk does not negotiate Kind as a logo. For the inspired-by ask: refused. For the fiction-adjacent ask: refused. For the thirty-day dark: refused.

He left the hyperscaler collar unanswered again. That silence was also a sentence.

Sending did not feel like victory. Sending felt like standing in heat with a packet that would come back thicker.

Lucid spoke once more.

“Outbound queued. Hostility markers will increase. I will not invent a better outcome.”

“Good,” AJ said.

He looked at the unopened stack under the demand — Antichristos mail that wanted a blessing he would not give for G, academy noise that wanted a degree G did not have, engineer mail that wanted keys and a face. Four quarters on one desk. He did not open them. Today’s weather was III. Consent about what shipped and what stayed was a later week’s hinge, not a morning’s surrender.

---

The second movement of the day was the body noticing what the sent folder had not fixed.

Shoulders climbed toward his ears and stayed there. He rolled them once, felt nothing release, left them where they were. The soft place under the sternum kept its bruise of good-morning death. He ate a piece of flatbread that had gone leather in the heat and tasted like nothing useful. He checked the solar node again — green — and the local node’s temperature — ordinary — and lucid.rodeo’s status — still green, still ordinary, still a tool that had kept its promise without needing a GPU apology.

A CongoNet badge pause notification refreshed. One brand had posted a careful note about “temporary distance while we evaluate adjacency.” Evaluate adjacency meant: wait to see if AJ knelt. AJ did not kneel. The badge stayed paused. CongoSky.cloud kept running itself the way a company that had already embodied Kind as operating law kept running when the street caught fire with the same sentence. The river desk did not need the badge to know what the company was.

He opened arjunabadger.press’s draft queue and stared at the half-made life. Mats. Hospital stigma waiting its careful return. Father dedication unread. Bouwer’s flat line already written and still dangerous. Clay pages not yet begun. The packet wanted all of that dark. Ritual distancing meant: pretend the friendship had been a branding error. Pretend open attribution had been a youthful excess. Pretend Lucid’s popularity had been a clean miracle with no source named.

AJ put his palm flat on the desk beside the keyboard. Wood and heat and the faint vibration of the truck’s systems. Empty twin pocket against his thigh. He did not perform grief for a knife that had gone where it belonged. He performed attention: the book continued; the demand was deferred hard; consent week was not today.

“Follow-up,” Lucid said, without drama. “Same demand cluster. Volume up. One partner restates badge pause. One hyperscaler salvage thread appends ‘book complicates optics.’ No new court finding. Cite-or-refuse: I can show the append, not the meeting that wrote it.”

“Leave it.”

“Acknowledged.”

He walked the truck again because motion was cheaper than rumination. At the open side he watched the fish eagle miss once and succeed once. At the dish he checked angle out of habit. At the fridge he poured water over his wrists and let the cold argue with the heat. Back at the desk the packet’s sent refusal sat like a stone. Stones do not become peace. Stones become weight you carry into the next week.

A human choir note arrived — not Lucid, a person on CongoNet who still practiced hymnbook work from somewhere with better air conditioning. Short. Exact. *Badge pauses noted. We are not darking lucid.rodeo. Kind ops unchanged. Writing?* AJ typed back: *Writing.* That was enough. The choir did not need a press conference from the river. CongoSky had never needed him to speechify about AI; the practice was shown working. Today the practice was: keep the node lit, keep the manuscript open, refuse the stop without pretending the cost was imaginary.

He almost opened the Antichristos stack by accident when his finger drifted. He stopped. Different hunger. Different day. The Kind tank outside did not need him to referee a sticker war in the inbox. The scraped stickers and the exact line could hold without his commentary.

He made a second coffee because the first had been punishment and the body still needed a tool. Hot this time. Bitter. He drank it standing at the open slit and watched a guide boat idle far enough out that faces were smudges. Someone on the boat pointed at Mutatus Maximus the way tourists point at anything that looks like a story. AJ did not wave. The truck was not a pilgrimage stop. It was a desk with wheels and a manuscript that Quarter III wanted dark.

Back at the chair he opened one partner notice again, not to negotiate, to inventory cost. Badge pause. Careful language. A sentence about loving CongoSky’s embodied Kind ops “in principle.” In principle meant: until the autobiography made the principle expensive. He forwarded the notice to the choir thread with three words — *noted, not kneeling* — and closed it. Cost stayed visible. Visibility was not the same as surrender.

Lucid spoke once, unprompted, which it rarely did without a cite.

“App-store fog note updated. Still no cited violation. Language now includes ‘ongoing review of community adjacency.’”

“Ongoing review means they want me to flinch first,” AJ said.

“Cite-or-refuse: I cannot confirm motive. I can confirm the language change.”

“That’s enough.”

He put both hands on the desk and felt the truck’s ordinary vibration travel up his arms. He had seeded the edge so the edge could stay lit without a hyperscaler parish. Today the quarrel wanted the edge to become a stage for repentance. He kept the stage dark and the node green.

Before afternoon fully claimed the metal he walked the rise again — not for the wrench this time, for the body. Dust. Heat. The node’s panel. He sat on his heels and let the scrub scratch his calves while a fish eagle worked the far bank without needing a mic. Stop-the-book was a sentence that wanted him smaller. Sitting still beside a lit thing he had woken from Mutatus Maximus was a sentence that kept him the size he was. He did not photograph the node. He did not draft a caption. He watched ants negotiate a cable run as if ToS weather were weather ants had never agreed to honour. Then he walked down and washed his hands and returned to the clay page with river smell still in his shirt.

Lucid spoke once more when he sat.

“Hostility markers up. Character unchanged. I will not invent that unchanged means safe.”

“Don’t,” AJ said. “Clay.”

He wrote three more sentences about Studio G light — hotel days, warehouse nights, the refusal to sand a life into respectability — while the packet’s demand stayed refused without being finished. The livestream script with blanks for personal reflection stayed closed. The inspired-by soft knife stayed refused. The fiction-adjacent ask stayed refused. Shoulders still high. Deferred hard no still a stone. That was the day’s true ledger.

---

Afternoon thickened. Heat did what heat does on the Chobe — made decisions feel heavier without making them clearer. AJ saved the mats page even though he had not advanced it. Saving was a way of telling the truck the book still existed. Then he typed a sentence he deleted: something about platforms and courage that sounded like a man auditioning for his own legend. Deleted. The packet wanted legend or repentance. He would give it neither.

“Next page,” he said. “Clay. Studio. Same man. Retrieve what you can cite from public sculpture record. Refuse invention.”

Lucid retrieved. Saatchi. studiog.co.za. Bay Hotel. Village N Life archive dates. Interview residue. Launch date sitting where public dates sit. No flourish.

AJ wrote the first sentences of clay slow, then less slow — hand under nails, pour, hotel light — while the packet’s demand stayed refused without being finished. The manuscript cursor moved. The river kept its indifference. The dish held. The node held. Hostility markers, Lucid had said, would increase. They did. A soft ping. Another. AJ did not open them. Opening them would have been a second conversation with a hunger that had already received its answer.

He paused once to drink water and once to look at the tank. Kind line. Scrapes. Heat shimmer. No sermon. CongoSky’s old operating law looking back at him from river paint while partner brands paused badges for adjacency. The joke, if it was a joke, did not need telling.

“Deferral status,” he said, testing the word.

“Outbound refusal stands. Consent language about autobiography shipment: not present in outbound. Stop-the-book demand: unanswered as acceptance; answered as refusal to stop on platform schedule. I will not invent that this resolves Quarter III.”

“It doesn’t,” AJ said. “It’s deferred hard.”

“Acknowledged. Deferred hard is not a legal term. It is accurate to your sent text.”

He almost smiled. Lucid’s refusals were character. So were its exactnesses. He did not need a cute collaborator. He needed a choir section that would not invent a quieter morning.

The third movement was smaller and meaner: a talent note that arrived dressed as admiration. *Loved what you built. We’re hiring edge mesh people who understand hymnbook discipline. Happy to fly someone to the regions for a conversation.* Regions meant: leave the river. Conversation meant: collar with better coffee. AJ deleted it. The snake on the desk did not need another friend.

He checked the manuscript word count without caring about the number. Caring about the number would have made the book a sprint again. He cared that the clay page had begun while the stop demand sat refused. That was the day’s true motion — not a victory, a turn.

Outside, scrub held. Inside, Mutatus Maximus kept being what it was: anti-yacht, matatu overkill, mobile DC, desk that held G’s life in progress while a quarter of the loud internet asked him to un-say a true sentence. AJ’s shoulders stayed high. The deferred hard no sat in the sent folder. Consent week would ask what ships and what stays. Today only asked whether he would kneel for quiet.

He would not.

Before the clay sentences fully took the afternoon, he did one more inventory of cost without opening the hostility folder. Badge pauses: three. Collar: unanswered. Antichristos: unread. Solar node: green. Manuscript: open. Shoulders: still high. Deferred hard no: still a stone in the sent folder, not a peace treaty. That inventory was the chapter’s true ledger — not a victory, a posture held in heat.

A new ping landed while he was still looking at the sent folder. Lucid spoke without drama.

“Follow-up hostility. Same demand cluster. No new court finding. Cite-or-refuse: I can show volume, not motive.”

“Leave it,” AJ said. “I’m writing.”

“Mats can wait,” he added. “Clay. Studio. Same man.”

Lucid retrieved what it had and waited.

AJ wrote.

The sentences of clay came with hotel light in them and Village N Life years under them and a man’s unpolished goals waiting their turn on the page — write natural philosophy and physics, smoke dab, talk shit — while the packet’s demand stayed refused without being finished. Outside, the scraped tank kept its Kind line. Inside, the dish held. The node held. The page took the next word, and the river kept its indifference, which was almost a mercy.

He paused once more before the light went copper and walked the open side with flatbread in hand — heat-food, river air, no toast to a refusal that was not victory. A guide boat idled far enough out that Mutatus Maximus looked like a story. He did not wave. The truck was a desk with wheels and a manuscript Quarter III wanted dark. He finished the flatbread, washed his hands, and returned to clay with the deferred hard no still a stone in the sent folder and the shoulders still high and the book still open. That was the day’s motion — not a speech, a posture held in heat while hostility markers increased and Lucid refused to invent a quieter morning.


# 14 — Clay and Constants

> **[I]** · inner autobiography · manuscript under AJ’s hand

---

Before the Constant had a public argument, I had clay under my nails.

Stellenbosch, 1980 — that is the birth year the bios keep. The body arrived first; the corpus arrived later; the academy’s preferred story prefers to begin at the ignored shelf because a shelf is tidy. Clay is not tidy. Clay remembers every thumb. Marble dust remembers the mix. GFRC remembers the day you got the pour wrong and the day you got it right enough to cast again.

I learned the body on mats — Judo first, then Jiu-Jitsu, the long apprenticeship of correct fall — and then I learned it again by modelling it: hard against soft, smooth against rough, growth against decay, permanent against temporary, realistic against abstract, classic against contemporary. Those juxtapositions are not a brand deck. They are what a hand discovers when it refuses to choose only one truth for a surface. On the mats, posture wrong meant someone smaller could end you. In the clay, posture wrong meant the figure lied about weight. Later, in the axioms, posture wrong meant a structure that looked clever and fell when measured. Same attention. Same refusal to waste motion.

The dojo smell is still under the studio smell if you know where to look — sweat-salt in the plaster, the particular quiet after a correct throw when both bodies agree the fall was fair. I did not become a sculptor to escape fighting arts. I became a sculptor because the same question kept arriving: where does weight go, and what happens when you lie about it. A hip joint that flatters the eye will betray the hand that has to hold the cast. A premise that flatters a room will betray the measurement that arrives later without applause.

I modelled in clay. I cast in a marble-and-GFRC composite I mixed myself — glass fibre, cement, marble dust arguing inside the same pour. Hard and soft in one object. People who later met me as a man with axioms liked to call the sculpture a hobby. It was never a hobby. It was the same attention that would later refuse to invent what the record did not support. You arrange weight so the fall is correct. You arrange mass so the figure holds. You arrange premises so the structure does not collapse. Same man.

The studio smells like dust and wet earth even on clean days. That smell is older than any inbox. It gets into the creases of the knuckles and stays. When people later asked how an economics graduate without a physics degree could derive what they preferred underived, I could have said: I already knew what happens when a surface pretends to be one thing. I did not say that in rooms that wanted a TED answer. I kept pouring.

Early clay taught me patience the mats had already started. You cannot rush a figure into honesty. You can rush a figure into looking finished — smooth where it should argue, soft where it should hold — and then the light arrives and exposes the lie. Hotel light is merciless in that way. So is measurement. So is a friend who writes your life without laundering the awkward rooms. I prefer the merciless teachers. They keep you from becoming a costume.

There is a afternoon light in Stellenbosch I still use as a private standard — not nostalgia, a calibration. When a figure looks honest in that light, it has a chance elsewhere. When it only looks honest under gallery spots, it is already lying. The Constant later asked for the same honesty under instruments instead of windows. I did not invent a new virtue for physics. I brought the old one into a colder room.

---

Village N Life came before Studio G had a name people could search.

I worked there until about mid-2005. Maree Brink mentored. Mentorship in that register is not a LinkedIn word; it is someone correcting your eye when the guest experience is lying about itself. I lived more than a year at Camps Bay Retreat — Earl’s Dyke then — with Hannetjie Brink, Maree’s late mother. A hotel is a theatre whether the guests admit it. Guests move through light and lawn and the promise of being briefly elsewhere. I began to think of sculpture as part of that theatre: not décor stuck in a corner, not a card with a shadow that does the wrong job, an anchor in the guest’s first breath of place. The Retreat taught me that atmosphere is not a slogan. It is where a body stands when it arrives.

Camps Bay light is a specific teacher. It does not flatter forever. It shows edges. Morning softens; afternoon argues; sunset tells the truth about mass. I learned to place work where the light would finish the sentence the clay had started. That is not mysticism. That is craft. The same craft later refused metaphor when a derivation needed a drop-result instead of a speech.

There were mornings at Earl’s Dyke when I walked the lawn before guests woke and practised seeing the theatre empty — no performance, only place. Empty theatre teaches you what an object must do without an audience applauding it into meaning. Skye would later need that lesson. Studio G would later need that lesson. The desk would later need that lesson when fame arrived sideways and tried to applaud the work into a costume. Empty lawn first. Weight second. Applause never.

Maree’s mentorship corrected the eye when a placement was décor pretending to be anchor. Hannetjie’s house corrected the life when a younger man mistook intensity for exactness. Village N Life until mid-2005 was the long apprenticeship in guest flow and atmosphere — not a brand story I recite for collectors, a set of rooms where I learned that bodies arrive carrying hope for elsewhere and will forgive many things except a false first breath of place.

I remember a Tuesday when a guest path had been routed past a figure that caught light wrong — the shadow made the belly look soft in a way the clay had not earned. Maree did not lecture. She moved the path three degrees. The figure became honest again. Three degrees. That is how most lies die if you are willing to move your feet instead of your mouth. Years later, when soft institutional weather arrived dressed as process, I recognised the opposite skill: people moving the path so the figure stays flattering and the truth never gets a clean angle.

Skye went onto a lawn at Camps Bay Retreat — feminine sculpture, sunset work, the kind of presence that does not need a label to do its job. People photographed her. People stood near her without knowing my life goals. That was fine. The work does not require a biography to hold weight. Antichristos, years later, would try to turn figures like her into relics. Quarter II would pretend the man who made her could not also derive. Both mistakes need me simpler than a pour.

I remember the install days more clearly than the praise. Scaffold. Level. The argument between lawn and plinth. A guest asking whether she was for sale while the light was still deciding her edges. Maree’s eye. Hannetjie’s house holding a younger man who had not yet been told, by institutions and by people who loved him badly, that he could not write anything of value. The sculpture held. The holding was the point.

Skye taught the guest experience what a brochure could not: that arrival can include weight. Camps Bay Retreat’s theatre was lawn and light and the sea’s argument beyond; she became part of that first breath. I did not stand beside her explaining juxtaposition. Juxtaposition was already in the surface — hard/soft, permanent/temporary — for anyone willing to look without a guide. Years later, when fame arrived sideways and people hunted relics, some would photograph her as if prophecy had always lived on that lawn. Prophecy is what you invent when craft embarrasses your categories. She was craft. She still is, wherever the lawn keeps her.

The Retreat years were apprenticeship in atmosphere. Earl’s Dyke then; Camps Bay Retreat in the telling that stuck. Mentorship from Maree; household gravity from Hannetjie. Village N Life until mid-2005 was not a corporate myth I polished for collectors. It was work: guest flow, place-making, the slow understanding that sculpture could be load-bearing in a hotel’s first impression without becoming lobby candy. When I left that chapter, the attention did not leave. It waited for Studio G to have walls.

---

Later I conceptualised Studio G at The Bay Hotel in Bloubergstrand — curated sculpture and painting for guests, a must-visit for collectors if the longer vision held, a sculpture garden at The Retreat if time and money and courage aligned. Short-term: unique contemporary art experiences at the hotels. Longer: the garden. Official launch of Studio G at The Bay: 27 July 2018. Village N Life partnership weather around that summer. Tourists about half the sales in those years, if the interview numbers still mean anything. Saatchi Art from 2017. studiog.co.za. My own site with the 2018 interview still sitting where interviews sit — Julia Strydom / Village N Life blog copy in the corporate archive, public record of a man who was already both gallery and desk.

Launch days are ordinary when you are inside them. Paint still drying somewhere it should not be. A figure catching the wrong reflection. Collectors arriving with the particular hunger of people who want to own weight. Hotel staff moving guests past curated work the way people move past anything that asks them to feel without a brochure lecture. I talked shit in the corners when the frame was wrong. I smoked when the day earned it. I did not sand those facts for the opening photographs. The photographs preferred cleaner surfaces. The life did not.

The night before the 27 July opening, humidity argued with a pour I had scheduled too confidently. Marble dust clumped. Fibre won a patch it should have shared. I stood in the studio with the failed section cooling and considered sanding the whole surface into the lie of smoothness so the morning guests would not see the quarrel. I did not. Hard edge stayed where hard belonged. Soft belly stayed soft. By noon the next day, people walked past a truth that did not need my speech, and I was already tired in the way only exactness tires you — not drama-tired, craft-tired.

Village N Life’s corporate archive still holds the interview weather and the grand-opening report — public, citeable, useful to anyone who needs a date. Useful is not the same as complete. Complete would include that humidity fight. Complete would include the friend who stood in the doorway without needing a tour. Complete would include the quiet after guests left when the room smelled of wine and dust and I knew the longer vision — sculpture garden at The Retreat — might never fully arrive, and that the short vision was already enough if I kept the route honest.

Bloubergstrand light differs from Camps Bay light the way two honest teachers differ: same subject, different angle. The Bay Hotel asked for curated sculpture and painting as guest experience — Studio G as room and route, not a closet of afterthoughts. Short-term vision: unique contemporary art experiences at the hotels. Longer: sculpture garden at The Retreat; Studio G as must-visit for collectors. Tourists about half the sales in those years. Collectors the other weather. Saatchi Art from 2017 sitting in parallel as another public shelf. None of that made me respectable enough for the academy’s preferred story. Respectability was never the goal. Exactness was.

There was a collector once who wanted the parable. I gave him the mix ratio instead. He left irritated and later bought a smaller piece through Saatchi without asking for myth. That is the transaction I trust: weight for money, not prophecy for applause. Antichristos would later reverse the trade and call it devotion. Quarter II would reverse it differently and call the clay proof I was unserious. Both trades prefer a simpler man than the pour allows.

---

The warehouse nights and the hotel days were one life. Cannabis bar and kill-switch dark. Talking shit and CODATA weather. A T-shirt that carried a sentence the walls would later spray everywhere: **Don’t be a cunt — be kind.** Guests at The Bay moved past curated work. Collectors came when they came. Tourists bought what the light made undeniable. I was not practising to become respectable. I was practising to stay exact — in clay, in conversation, in the long work that would later be called natural philosophy by people who needed a category and physics by people who needed a fight.

Studio G’s longer vision — sculpture garden at The Retreat, collectors treating the hotel rooms as a route — was never separate from the desk where axioms got installed. People who only saw one room missed the pour. The Bay’s official opening date sits in Village N Life’s corporate archive the way public dates sit: available, citeable, insufficient. A date does not tell you about the mix going wrong at midnight. A date does not tell you about the friend whose sudden death sits in the remembering posts on my site — James van Niekerk, friend not brother, grief with a different address than Bouwer’s boardroom. Living-person care: I name the mark and do not make a thriller of either. The studio kept working. The desk kept working. That is not toughness. That is the arrangement after the fall is already correct enough to stand up again.

GFRC is an argument you win with patience. Marble dust wants one thing; fibre wants another; cement holds the quarrel. I developed the composite because the figures needed hard and soft in one cast, not a surface apologising for itself. When engineers later asked for keys to stacks built on Code rails, I recognised the hunger: they wanted a clean interface. Interfaces are useful. Interfaces are also how a life gets flattened into a product face. Sculpture had already taught me that a clean interface can be a lie if the weight underneath is wrong.

I keep a scrap of failed pour somewhere the guests never see — not as relic, as teacher. The failure shows where fibre won too hard and marble dust lost the argument. Looking at it steadies the hand before a new mix. Looking at a bad frame in conversation steadies the mouth before talk shit. Looking at a premise that wants to be clever steadies the derivation before it falls in public. Same scrap logic. Same man.

On good studio days the body forgets the inbox. Hands in clay. Dust on the floor. A figure’s belly soft where the world expects armour; a shoulder hard where the world expects comfort. Juxtaposition as method, not brand. The academy that preferred me silent liked to point at the studio as proof of unseriousness — as if a man who understands mass in one room cannot understand mass in another. Antichristos liked to point at the studio as proof of prophecy — as if a feminine sculpture on a Camps Bay lawn were a relic waiting for liturgy. Both hungers are the same hunger: they need a single surface.

I kept two surfaces. I kept them as one attention.

The warehouse had its own light — less flattering than Camps Bay, more honest about dust. Cannabis bar weather and kill-switch dark could share a night without becoming a brand deck. Someone would ask for the elevator version of the work; I would talk shit until the elevator gave up. Someone would ask whether kindness was a personality trait; the T-shirt already carried the structural claim: **Don’t be a cunt — be kind.** CongoSky would later embody that as company law; walls would catch fire; I would refuse ownership of the religion that wanted a face. The warehouse night did not know it was rehearsing 2035 weather. It only knew exactness and smoke and the refusal to sand a life into respectability.

There were nights when the axioms sat open on a laptop with clay dust on the keys, and the figure on the stand waited, and neither room won. That is the arrangement people keep trying to split for me: gallery or desk, artist or crank, soft man or hard theorem. Split is how you make a usable lie. Unsplit is how you keep a life from collapsing under its own false categories.

---

My stated life goals are not a brand.

Write natural philosophy and physics.

Smoke dab.

Talk shit.

I keep them unpolished because polishing is how a quarter of the planet turns a man into a usable lie. The academy hears dab and reaches for the hospital file. The faith that wants a face hears talk shit and tries to sand it into parable. The engineers who want keys hear natural philosophy and ask for a signature on a lab door. I am still the man who said those three goals out loud because they are true. The mats are under them. The clay is under them. The GFRC pour is under them. The ignored shelf is under them. The unread father dedication is under them — entire corpus pointed at a man who has read none of it. Bouwer is under them — Saxonwold, Friday 5 September 2025, insolvency practice, public record wound, consultation pretext, boardroom — and that page is not this page’s thriller. This page is clay and constants in the same life. The wound travels forward without being solved.

Talk shit is not a slogan for cruelty. It is the refusal to let a false frame stand because the room prefers manners. Dab is not a footnote. It is part of the arrangement that keeps the work from becoming a costume. Write natural philosophy and physics is the long labour that does not care whether Quarter II has finished singing about credentials.
